C=C(\epsilon)>0
such that for all
v\in\partial B(z^{\delta},\epsilon)
, and

s\in[0,t^{\delta}-c]
, and for every
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
, we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{v}[\mathcal{R}(s)=z^{\delta}]=\;

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{v}[\mathcal{R}(s)=z^{\delta},\,T^{\delta}_{j}<\infty,\textnormal{ and }S^{\delta}_{j}=\infty]

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\mathsf{E}^{\delta}_{v}\Big[[\mathbb{1}{\{T_{j}^{\delta}<\infty\}}\,\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{\mathcal{R}(T_{j}^{\delta})}\big[\mathcal{R}(s-T_{j}^{\delta})=z^{\delta}\textnormal{ and }\mathcal{R}[0,s-T_{j}^{\delta}]\subset B(z^{\delta},\epsilon)\big]\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{v}[T_{j}^{\delta}<\infty]\;\max_{u\in[0,t^{\delta}-c]}\max_{w\in\partial B(z^{\delta},\epsilon/2)}\hat{\mathsf{P}}^{\delta}_{w}[\hat{\mathcal{R}}(u)=z^{\delta}]

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle C\delta^{2}\,\exp\Big(\!-\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2(t^{\delta}-c)}\Big).\qquad\textnormal{[by the local central limit theorem and~\eqref{eqn::exp_time}]}

(2.24)

We can conclude by the following two estimates:

•

when
\mathcal{R}(c)\in B(z^{\delta},\frac{\epsilon}{2})
, we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{z^{\delta}}\Big[\max_{s\in[c,t^{\delta}]}|\mathcal{R}(s)-z^{\delta}|>\epsilon,\,\mathcal{R}(c)\in B(z^{\delta},\tfrac{\epsilon}{2}),\textnormal{ and }\mathcal{R}(t^{\delta})=z^{\delta}\Big]\leq\;

\displaystyle\max_{s\in[0,t^{\delta}-c]}\max_{v\in\partial B(z^{\delta},\epsilon)}\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{v}[\mathcal{R}(s)=z^{\delta}];

•

when
\mathcal{R}(c)\notin B(z^{\delta},\frac{\epsilon}{2})
, we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{z^{\delta}}\Big[\max_{s\in[c,t^{\delta}]}|\mathcal{R}(s)-z^{\delta}|>\epsilon,\,\mathcal{R}(c)\notin B(z^{\delta},\tfrac{\epsilon}{2}),\textnormal{ and }\mathcal{R}(t^{\delta})=z^{\delta}\Big]\leq\;

\displaystyle\max_{s\in[0,t^{\delta}-c]}\max_{v\in\partial B(z^{\delta},\epsilon/2)}\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{v}[\mathcal{R}(s)=z^{\delta}].

Combining this with (
2.3
) and the convergence of
\delta^{-2}\,\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{z^{\delta}}[\mathcal{R}(t^{\delta})=z^{\delta}]
, we obtain (
2.23
).
∎

Next, we control the lifetimes
t_{\ell}
of loops
\ell
under
\Lambda_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{ND}}
.
For every
c>0
, we denote the
c
-neighbourhood of the inner boundary
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega^{\delta}
by
\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c)}:=\{v\in\mathcal{V}^{\delta}\;|\;\dist(v,\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega^{\delta})<c\}
.

Lemma 2.7
.

For every
\varepsilon>0
, there exist
\delta_{0}=\delta_{0}(\varepsilon)>0
and
a constant
M=M(\varepsilon)>1
such that
\smash{\underset{\varepsilon\to 0}{\lim}\,M(\varepsilon)\to\infty}
and

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{ND}}\big[\ell\in\mathcal{L}_{*}^{\delta}\textnormal{ and its lifetime }t_{\ell}\notin\big[\tfrac{1}{M},M\big]\big]<\varepsilon,\qquad\textnormal{for all }\delta\in(0,\delta_{0}).

(2.25)

Furthermore, there exists a constant
c=c(\varepsilon)>0
such that

\smash{\underset{\varepsilon\to 0}{\lim}\,c(\varepsilon)\to 0}
and

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{ND}}\big[\ell\in\mathcal{L}_{*}^{\delta}\textnormal{ and the root of }\ell\textnormal{ lies in }\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c)}\big]<\varepsilon,\qquad\textnormal{for all }\delta\in(0,\delta_{0}).

(2.26)

Moreover, the estimates
(
2.25
,
2.26
)
are uniform with respect to the shape of the domains
\Omega^{\delta}
.

To prove Lemma
2.7
, we need two auxiliary results.

•

In Lemma
2.8
, we bound the discrete Green’s function
\smash{\mathrm{G}_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{ND}}}
on
\Omega^{\delta}
with Dirichlet boundary condition on
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega^{\delta}
and Neumann boundary condition on
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega^{\delta}
, defined as

\displaystyle\mathrm{G}_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{ND}}(z,w):=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{z}[\mathcal{R}(j)=w\textnormal{ and }\tau_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}^{\delta}>j],\qquad z,w\in\mathcal{V}^{\delta},

where
\tau_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}^{\delta}
is the hitting time at
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega^{\delta}
of the random walk
\mathcal{R}\sim\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{z}=\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{z}[\,\cdot\,|\,\mathcal{R}(0)=z]
.

•

In Lemma
2.9
, we bound the measure under
\Lambda_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{ND}}
of loops in
\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c)}
with non-trivial winding:
it is very unlikely for small loops to have winding even with Neumann boundary condition on
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega^{\delta}
.

Lemma 2.8
.

For every
c>0
, there exist
\delta_{0}=\delta_{0}(c)>0
and a constant
C=C(c)>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathrm{G}_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{ND}}(z,w)\leq C,\qquad\textnormal{for all }z,w\in\mathcal{V}^{\delta}\textnormal{ with }|z-w|\geq c\textnormal{ and }w\notin\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c)},\textnormal{ and for all }\delta\in(0,\delta_{0}).

Moreover, the estimate is uniform with respect to the shape of the domains
\Omega^{\delta}
, provided that
\Omega^{\delta}\subset B(0,R)
.

Proof.

Suppose
\Omega^{\delta}\subset B(0,R)
for some
R>0
. Fix
w\in\mathcal{V}^{\delta}\setminus\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c)}
.
We know that
\mathrm{G}_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{ND}}(\cdot,w)|_{\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega^{\delta}}
takes its maximum at some
v_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\in\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega^{\delta}
,
and
\mathrm{G}_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{ND}}(\cdot,w)|_{\Omega^{\delta}\setminus B(w,\tfrac{c}{2})}
takes its maximum at some
v_{c}\in\partial B(w,\tfrac{c}{2})
.

On the one hand, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{G}_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{ND}}(v_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}},w)\leq\;

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{v_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}}\big[\mathcal{R}\textnormal{ hits }\partial B(w,\tfrac{c}{2})\textnormal{ before hitting }\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega^{\delta}\big]\max_{u^{\delta}\in\partial B(w,\tfrac{c}{2})}\mathrm{G}_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{ND}}(u^{\delta},w)

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle(1-q)\,\mathrm{G}_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{ND}}(v_{c},w),

where
0<q=q(c)<1
and
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
(uniformly on
\Omega^{\delta}
subject to the above conditions).
Hence, we have

\displaystyle q\,\mathrm{G}_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{ND}}(v_{c},w)\leq\mathrm{G}_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{ND}}(v_{c},w)-\mathrm{G}_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{ND}}(v_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}},w).

(2.27)

On the other hand, by the strong Markov property, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{G}_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{ND}}(v_{c},w)-\mathrm{G}_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{D}(v_{c},w)\leq\mathrm{G}_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{ND}}(v_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}},w),

(2.28)

where
\mathrm{G}^{\textnormal{D}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}
is the discrete Green’s function on
\Omega^{\delta}
with Dirichlet boundary condition on
\partial\Omega^{\delta}
, defined as

\displaystyle\mathrm{G}_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{D}}(z,w):=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{z}[\hat{\mathcal{R}}(j)=w\textnormal{ and }\hat{\tau}^{\delta}>j],\qquad z,w\in\mathcal{V}^{\delta},

with
\hat{\tau}^{\delta}
the hitting time at
\partial\Omega^{\delta}
of the standard random walk
\hat{\mathcal{R}}
on
\delta\mathbb{Z}^{2}
.
Combining (
2.27
,
2.28
) and using the strong Markov property, we find that

\displaystyle\mathrm{G}_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{ND}}(z,w)\leq\mathrm{G}_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{ND}}(v_{c},w)\leq\frac{1}{q}\,\mathrm{G}_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{D}}(v_{c},w).

The claim now follows from a standard bound for
\mathrm{G}_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{D}}

(for instance, see
[
CS11
, Theorem 2.5]
; this bound is uniform on
\Omega^{\delta}
subject to the above conditions by domain monotonicity of the Green’s function with Dirichlet boundary conditions).
∎

Lemma 2.9
.

For every
\varepsilon>0
, there exist
\delta_{0}=\delta_{0}(\varepsilon)>0
and a constant
c_{0}=c_{0}(\varepsilon)>0
such that

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{ND}}\big[\ell\in\mathcal{L}_{*}^{\delta}\textnormal{ such that }\ell\subset\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c)}\big]<\varepsilon,\qquad 0<c\leq c_{0},\qquad\textnormal{for all }\delta\in(0,\delta_{0}).

Proof.

We bound the left-hand side by controlling the variance of the winding
K(\mathcal{R})
of a random walk
\mathcal{R}\sim\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{x,v,c}
starting from
x=x_{1}^{\delta}
, conditioned to leave
\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c)}
through a given inner vertex
v\in\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c)}
on the outer boundary of
\Omega^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c)}:=\Omega^{\delta}\cap\tilde{\Omega}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c)}
,
where
\tilde{\Omega}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c)}:=\textnormal{Int}({\underset{v\in\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c)}}{\cup}}\,\overline{f(v)})
, and stopped there.

Let
\Gamma=\mathrm{LE}(\mathcal{R})
be the loop-erasure of
\mathcal{R}
.
By
[
LL10
, Proposition 9.5.1]
, we have

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\textnormal{ND}}_{\Omega^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c)}}\big[(\varphi(\ell))^{2}\,\mathbb{1}_{\{\varphi(\ell)\neq 0\}}]\;=\;4\pi^{2}\big(\textnormal{Var}_{x,v,c}[K(\mathcal{R})]-\textnormal{Var}_{x,v,c}[K(\Gamma)|]\big)\;\leq\;4\pi^{2}\textnormal{Var}_{x,v,c}[K(\mathcal{R})].

By Markov’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{ND}}\big[\ell\in\mathcal{L}_{*}^{\delta}\textnormal{ such that }\ell\subset\Omega^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c)}\big]\leq\textnormal{Var}_{x,v,c}[K(\mathcal{R})],

and by taking sum over
v
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\Omega_{\delta}}^{ND}\big[\ell\in\mathcal{L}_{*}^{\delta}\textnormal{ such that }\ell\subset\Omega^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c)}\big]\leq 4\pi^{2}\textnormal{Var}[K(\mathcal{R})].

Thus, to conclude, it suffices to prove that for every
\varepsilon^{\prime}>0
, we can choose
c
small enough such that

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{x}\big[|K(\mathcal{R})|\geq j\big]\leq(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{j},\qquad j\geq 1,

(2.29)

uniformly in
x\in\Omega^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c)}
. Consider two crosscuts
\varrho_{1},\varrho_{2}
connecting the two boundary components of
\Omega^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c)}
and such that
they are at macroscopic distance away from each other and from
x
.
If the LERW
\Gamma
has non-trivial winding, it must cross both crosscuts.
Hence, we consider stopping times for the random walk
\mathcal{R}
, defined recursively as

T_{1}:=\inf\{t\geq 0\colon\mathcal{R}(t)\in\varrho_{1}\}
;
and
S_{j}:=\inf\{t\geq T_{j}\colon\mathcal{R}(t)\in\varrho_{2}\}
;
and
T_{j+1}:=\inf\{t\geq S_{j}\colon\mathcal{R}(t)\in\varrho_{1}\}
, for
j\geq 1
.
Now, using the the discrete Beurling estimate and the strong Markov property of
\mathcal{R}
, we see that

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{x}\big[|K(\mathcal{R})|\geq j\big]\leq\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{x}[0<T_{1}<\cdots<S_{j-1}<\infty]\leq(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{j},

whenever
c=c(\varepsilon^{\prime})
and
\delta_{0}=\delta_{0}(\varepsilon^{\prime})>0
are small enough.
This implies the asserted estimate.
∎

We are now ready to finish with Lemma
2.7
.

Proof of Lemma
2.7
.

Fix
\varepsilon>0
.
To prove the first asserted estimate (
2.25
), thanks to Lemma
2.9
it will be sufficient to consider loops exiting a neighbourhood of the inner boundary
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega^{\delta}
(cf. (
2.30
)).
Denote
c_{\Omega}:=\tfrac{1}{100}\dist(\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega,\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega)
. For
c\in(0,c_{\Omega})
, we define two stopping times for the random walk
\mathcal{R}
on
\Omega^{\delta}
:

•

if
\mathcal{R}(0)\notin\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c)}
, then we set
\sigma_{1}=\sigma_{1}(c):=0
;

•

if
\mathcal{R}(0)\in\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c)}
, then we set
\sigma_{1}=\sigma_{1}(c)
to be the first time when the walk

\mathcal{R}
exits
\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(2c)}
; and

\displaystyle\sigma_{2}=\sigma_{2}(c):=\inf\{t\geq 0\colon|\mathcal{R}(\sigma_{2})-\mathcal{R}(\sigma_{1})|\geq c/2\}.

In both cases, we have
\min\{|\mathcal{R}(\sigma_{2})-\mathcal{R}(\sigma_{1})|,\,|\mathcal{R}(\sigma_{2})-\mathcal{R}(0)|\}\geq c/2

and
\mathcal{R}(\sigma_{2})\notin\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c/2)}
.
Note that any uncontractible loop
\ell\not\subset\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(2c)}
has diameter at least
c
.
With Lemma
2.9
at hand,
the crucial claim is that for each
\varepsilon^{\prime}>0
, we can choose
M^{\prime}=M^{\prime}(\varepsilon^{\prime},c)>1
large enough and
\delta_{0}^{\prime}=\delta_{0}^{\prime}(\varepsilon^{\prime})>0
small enough
such that

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{ND}}\big[\ell\not\subset\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(2c)},\,\diam(\ell)\geq c,\textnormal{ and its lifetime }t_{\ell}\notin\big[\tfrac{1}{M^{\prime}},M^{\prime}\big]\big]\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}\;\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\notin\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c/2)}\\
|w-z|\geq c/2\end{subarray}}\mathrm{G}^{\textnormal{ND}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}(z,w),

(2.30)

for all
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0}^{\prime})
(where the maximum of the Green’s function is controlled by Lemma
2.8
).
Note that

\displaystyle\;\Lambda_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{ND}}\big[\ell\not\subset\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(2c)},\,\diam(\ell)\geq c,\textnormal{ and its lifetime }t_{\ell}\notin\big[\tfrac{1}{M^{\prime}},M^{\prime}\big]\big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\sum_{z\in\mathcal{V}^{\delta}\setminus\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\mathcal{V}^{\delta}}\frac{\delta^{2}}{2}\sum_{t\in\frac{1}{2}\delta^{2}\mathbb{N}\setminus[\frac{1}{M^{\prime}},M^{\prime}]}\frac{1}{t}\,\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{z}\big[\mathcal{R}[0,t]\not\subset\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(2c)},\,\diam\mathcal{R}[0,t]\geq c,\textnormal{ and }\mathcal{R}(t)=z\big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\sum_{z\in\mathcal{V}^{\delta}\setminus\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\mathcal{V}^{\delta}}\frac{\delta^{2}}{2}\sum_{t\in\frac{1}{2}\delta^{2}\mathbb{N}\setminus[\frac{1}{M^{\prime}},M^{\prime}]}\frac{1}{t}\,\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{z}\big[0\leq\sigma_{1}<\sigma_{2}<t\textnormal{ and }\mathcal{R}(t)=z\big].

We split the sum over
t
into the following two parts.

•

For
t\geq M^{\prime}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{z\in\mathcal{V}^{\delta}\setminus\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\mathcal{V}^{\delta}}\frac{\delta^{2}}{2}\sum_{t\in\frac{1}{2}\delta^{2}\mathbb{N}\cap[M^{\prime},\infty)}\frac{1}{t}\,\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{z}\big[0\leq\sigma_{1}<\sigma_{2}<t\textnormal{ and }\mathcal{R}(t)=z\big]

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\frac{1}{M^{\prime}}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{V}^{\delta}\setminus\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\mathcal{V}^{\delta}}\frac{\delta^{2}}{2}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\notin\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c/2)}\\
|w-z|\geq\frac{c}{2}\end{subarray}}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{w}[\mathcal{R}(j)=z]

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\frac{1}{M^{\prime}}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{V}^{\delta}\setminus\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\mathcal{V}^{\delta}}\frac{\delta^{2}}{2}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\notin\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c/2)}\\
|w-z|\geq\frac{c}{2}\end{subarray}}\mathrm{G}^{\textnormal{ND}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}(w,z)\;\leq\;\frac{\Leb(\tilde{\Omega}^{\delta})}{2M^{\prime}}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\notin\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c/2)}\\
|w-z|\geq\frac{c}{2}\end{subarray}}\mathrm{G}^{\textnormal{ND}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}(w,z),

(2.31)

where
\Leb(\tilde{\Omega}^{\delta})
is the Lebesgue measure of the domain
7
7

7

Note that
\Leb(\tilde{\Omega}^{\delta})
is uniformly controlled by
\Leb(\Omega)
by the assumption in Item
2
of the Setup, when
\delta
is small.

\tilde{\Omega}^{\delta}:=\textnormal{Int}(\smash{\underset{v\in\mathcal{V}^{\delta}}{\cup}}\,\overline{f(v)})\subset\mathbb{C}
.

•

For
t\leq\frac{1}{M^{\prime}}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{z\in\mathcal{V}^{\delta}\setminus\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\mathcal{V}^{\delta}}\frac{\delta^{2}}{2}\sum_{t\in\frac{1}{2}\delta^{2}\mathbb{N}\cap(0,\frac{1}{M^{\prime}}]}\frac{1}{t}\,\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{z}\big[0\leq\sigma_{1}<\sigma_{2}<t\textnormal{ and }\mathcal{R}(t)=z\big]

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\sum_{z\in\mathcal{V}^{\delta}\setminus\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\mathcal{V}^{\delta}}\frac{\delta^{2}}{2}\,\mathsf{E}^{\delta}_{z}\bigg[\frac{\mathbb{1}{\{0\leq\sigma_{1}<\sigma_{2}<\tfrac{1}{M^{\prime}}\}}}{\sigma_{2}-\sigma_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{\mathcal{R}(\sigma_{2})}\big[\mathcal{R}(j)=z\big]\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\frac{\Leb(\tilde{\Omega}^{\delta})}{2}\max_{z\in\mathcal{V}^{\delta}}\mathsf{E}^{\delta}_{z}\bigg[\frac{\mathbb{1}{\{0\leq\sigma_{1}<\sigma_{2}<\tfrac{1}{M^{\prime}}\}}}{\sigma_{2}-\sigma_{1}}\bigg]\,\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\notin\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c/2)}\\
|w-z|\geq\frac{c}{2}\end{subarray}}\mathrm{G}^{\textnormal{ND}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}(w,z).

(2.32)

Let us denote by
\hat{\mathcal{R}}\sim\hat{\mathsf{P}}^{\delta}_{z}=\hat{\mathsf{P}}^{\delta}_{z}[\,\cdot\,|\,\hat{\mathcal{R}}(0)=z]

the standard random walk on
\delta\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, and by
T_{c}
the first time when
\hat{\mathcal{R}}
exits
\partial B(0,c/2)
.
Combining with
[
LL10
, Proposition 2.1.2(b)]

we find that there exists two constants
K_{1}>0
and
K_{2}>0
such that for each
z\in\mathcal{V}^{\delta}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}^{\delta}_{z}\bigg[\frac{\mathbb{1}{\{0\leq\sigma_{1}<\sigma_{2}<\tfrac{1}{M^{\prime}}\}}}{\sigma_{2}-\sigma_{1}}\bigg]\leq\hat{\mathsf{E}}^{\delta}_{0}\bigg[\frac{\mathbb{1}{\{T_{c}<\tfrac{1}{M^{\prime}}\}}}{T_{c}}\bigg]\leq K_{1}\,\exp\Big(\!-K_{2}\,c^{2}\,M^{\prime}\Big).

(2.33)

Plugging (
2.33
) into (
2.32
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{z\in\mathcal{V}^{\delta}\setminus\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\mathcal{V}^{\delta}}\frac{\delta^{2}}{2}\sum_{t\in\frac{1}{2}\delta^{2}\mathbb{N}\cap(0,\frac{1}{M^{\prime}}]}\frac{1}{t}\,\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{z}\big[0\leq\sigma_{1}<\sigma_{2}<t\textnormal{ and }\mathcal{R}(t)=z\big]

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\frac{\Leb(\tilde{\Omega}^{\delta})}{2}\,K_{1}\,\exp\Big(\!-K_{2}\,c^{2}\,M^{\prime}\Big)\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\notin\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c/2)}\\
|w-z|\geq\frac{c}{2}\end{subarray}}\mathrm{G}^{\textnormal{ND}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}(w,z).

(2.34)

By choosing
M^{\prime}
large enough and
\delta_{0}^{\prime}
small enough, these two estimates imply (
2.30
).
Now, the asserted estimate (
2.25
) follows
taking
c=(c_{0}/2)\wedge c_{\Omega}
with
c_{0}=c_{0}(\varepsilon/2)>0
as in Lemma
2.9
, and

\varepsilon^{\prime}=\varepsilon/(2C(c/2))
with
C=C(c/2)
as in Lemma
2.8
,
and choosing
\delta_{0}=\delta_{0}(\varepsilon)>0
suitably small.

It remains to prove (
2.26
).
Again, thanks to Lemma
2.9
we only consider loops exiting a neighbourhood of the inner boundary
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega^{\delta}
.
Moreover, thanks to Equation (
2.25
), we may restrict to lifetimes in
\big(\tfrac{1}{M},M\big)
,
where
M=M(\varepsilon)
and
\delta_{0}=\delta_{0}(\varepsilon)
are suitably chosen.
As in (
•
‣
2.3
,
2.32
), we obtain

\displaystyle\;\Lambda_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{ND}}\big[\ell\not\subset\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(2c)},\,\diam(\ell)\geq c,\textnormal{ its lifetime }t_{\ell}\in\big(\tfrac{1}{M},M\big),\textnormal{ and its root lies in }\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c^{\prime})}\big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;M\sum_{z\in\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c^{\prime})}}\frac{\delta^{2}}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\mathsf{E}^{\delta}_{z}\big[\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{\mathcal{R}(\sigma_{2})}\big[\mathcal{R}(j)=z\big]\big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\;\frac{M}{2}\,\Leb(\tilde{\Omega}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c^{\prime})})\;\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\notin\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c/2)}\\
|w-z|\geq\frac{c}{2}\end{subarray}}\mathrm{G}^{\textnormal{ND}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}(z,w),\qquad\;c^{\prime}\in(0,c),

(2.35)

where
8
8

8

Note that the Lebesgue measure is uniformly controlled by the Lebesgue measure of the corresponding neighbourhood for the inner boundary of
\Omega
, by the assumption in Item
2
of the Setup, when
\delta
is small.

\tilde{\Omega}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c^{\prime})}:=\textnormal{Int}(\smash{\underset{v\in\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c^{\prime})}}{\cup}}\,\overline{f(v)})\subset\mathbb{C}
.
Choosing
c^{\prime}
small enough, (
2.26
) follows.
∎

Combining Proposition
2.6
and Lemma
2.7
together, we can now deduce the asymptotics of the loop soup terms in Proposition
2.3
. This is encapsulated by the following result.

Lemma 2.10
.

For any
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to 0}\Lambda_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{ND}}\big[\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\beta\varphi(\ell)}\,\mathbb{1}{\{\ell\in\mathcal{L}_{*}^{\delta}\}}\big]=\;

\displaystyle\mu_{\Omega}^{\textnormal{ND}}\big[\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\beta\varphi(\ell)}\mathbb{1}{\{\ell\in\mathcal{L}_{*}\}}\big].

Proof.

For
\varepsilon>0
, take
c=c(\varepsilon)
, and
M=M(\varepsilon)
,
and
\delta_{0}=\delta_{0}(\varepsilon)
as in Lemma
2.7
.
Consider the set

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{*}^{\delta}(M,c):=\Big\{\ell\in\mathcal{L}_{*}^{\delta}\;|\;\textnormal{ the lifetime }t^{\delta}\in\big(\tfrac{1}{M},M\big),\textnormal{ and the root of }\ell\textnormal{ lies not in }\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c)}\Big\}

of discrete loops, and define
\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c)}:=\{z\in\Omega\;|\;\dist(z,\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega)<c\}
.
For
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to 0}\Lambda_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{ND}}\big[\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\beta\varphi(\ell)}\,\mathbb{1}{\{\mathcal{A}_{*}^{\delta}(M,c)\}}\big]=\;

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to 0}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}z^{\delta}\in\mathcal{V}^{\delta}\setminus\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\mathcal{V}^{\delta}\\
z^{\delta}\not\in\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c)}\end{subarray}}\frac{\delta^{2}}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t^{\delta}\in\frac{1}{2}\delta^{2}\mathbb{N}\\
\frac{1}{M}\leq t^{\delta}\leq M\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{t^{\delta}}\,\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{z^{\delta}}\Big[\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\beta\varphi(\ell)}\,\mathbb{1}{\{\ell\in\mathcal{L}_{*}^{\delta},\,\mathcal{R}(t^{\delta})=z^{\delta}\}}\Big]

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\setminus\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c)}}\mathrm{d}z\int_{[\tfrac{1}{M},M]}\mathrm{d}t\;\frac{p^{\textnormal{ND}}_{\Omega}(z,z,t)}{t}\;\mu^{\textnormal{ND}}_{\Omega}(z,z,t)\big[\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\beta\varphi(\ell)}\,\mathbb{1}{\{\ell\in\mathcal{L}_{*}\}}\big],

by Proposition
2.6
, Lemma
2.7
, and (
2.21
).
Taking
\varepsilon\to 0
, so
M(\varepsilon)\to\infty
and
c(\varepsilon)\to 0
, we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to 0}\Lambda_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{ND}}\big[\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\beta\varphi(\ell)}\mathbb{1}{\{\ell\in\mathcal{L}_{*}^{\delta}\}}\big]=\;

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\mathrm{d}z\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}t\;\frac{p^{\textnormal{ND}}_{\Omega}(z,z,t)}{t}\;\mu^{\textnormal{ND}}_{\Omega}(z,z,t)\big[\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\beta\varphi(\ell)}\mathbb{1}{\{\ell\in\mathcal{L}_{*}\}}\big]

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\mu_{\Omega}^{\textnormal{ND}}\big[\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\beta\varphi(\ell)}\mathbb{1}{\{\ell\in\mathcal{L}_{*}\}}\big],\qquad\beta\in\mathbb{R}.\qquad\textnormal{[by definition~\eqref{eqn::mixed_loop}]}

This completes the proof.
∎

To complete the proof of Theorem
1.1
, it remains to deal with the ratio of determinants in the formulas in Proposition
2.3
.
The asymptotics will be obtained in Lemma
2.11
.

Recall that we denote by
\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}^{\delta}=(\mathcal{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}^{\delta},\mathcal{E}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}^{\delta})
the universal cover of the graph
\Omega^{\delta}
, obtained by gluing its copies
\{\Omega^{\delta}_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}=\{(\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{m},\mathcal{E}^{\delta}_{m})\}_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}
together along the cut
\zeta
.
In particular, there are bijections
\mathcal{V}^{\delta}\leftrightarrow\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{m}
and
\mathcal{E}^{\delta}\leftrightarrow\mathcal{E}^{\delta}_{m}
given by
v\leftrightarrow v_{m}
and
e\leftrightarrow e_{m}
, and
h^{\delta}_{\beta}(\cdot,v)
defined in (
2.2
) defines a discrete harmonic function on
\mathcal{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}^{\delta}
.
For each subset
V\subset\mathcal{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}^{\delta}
of vertices and for each vertex
x\in\mathcal{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}^{\delta}
, define

\displaystyle h^{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}(x,V):=\mathbb{P}[\textnormal{ random walk on }\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}^{\delta}\textnormal{ starting from }x\textnormal{ hits }V\textnormal{ before hitting }\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}^{\delta}\setminus V],

which is discrete harmonic on
\mathcal{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}^{\delta}
with
h^{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}(\cdot,V)=1
on
V

and
h^{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}(\cdot,V)=0
(elsewhere) on the outer boundary.
When
V=\{v\}
is a singleton with
v\in\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\mathcal{E}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}^{\delta}
, we simply denote

h^{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}(x,\{v\})=h^{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}(x,v)
.

Let
\phi_{r}\colon\Omega\to\mathbb{A}_{r}
be the conformal isomorphism onto the annulus

\mathbb{A}_{r}=A(r,1)
,
so that
\phi_{r}
coincides with the restriction to
\Omega
of the conformal isomorphism

\phi\colon\Omega\to\mathbb{D}
used above. The universal cover of
\mathbb{A}_{r}
is the strip

S_{r}:=\{z=x+\mathrm{i}y\in\mathbb{C}\,|\,x\in\mathbb{R},\,0<y<-\log r\}
, with covering map is given by
p(z):=e^{-\mathrm{i}z+\log r}
for
z\in S_{r}
. Denote by
\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}
the universal cover of
\Omega
obtained by gluing
\Omega
with its copies along
\zeta:=\phi_{r}^{-1}[r,1]
, and by
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}
its outer boundary.
Finally, let
\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}_{r}\colon\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}\to S_{r}
be the associated lift of
\phi_{r}
.
Denote by
\mathrm{H}^{\textnormal{ND}}_{S_{r}}
the
Poisson kernel
on
S_{r}
with Dirichlet boundary condition on
\mathbb{R}-\mathrm{i}\log r
and Neumann boundary condition on
\mathbb{R}
: the function
\mathrm{H}^{\textnormal{ND}}_{S_{r}}(\cdot,\cdot)
is smooth on the set

\displaystyle(\overline{S_{r}}\times\overline{S_{r}})\setminus\{(z,z)\;|\;z\in\mathbb{R}-\mathrm{i}\log r\};

for every
z\in\mathbb{R}-\mathrm{i}\log r
, the function
\mathrm{H}^{\textnormal{ND}}_{S_{r}}(\cdot,z)
is harmonic on
S_{r}
, and

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{\textnormal{ND}}_{S_{r}}(\cdot,z)|_{(\mathbb{R}-\mathrm{i}\log r)\setminus\{z\}}=0\qquad\textnormal{and}\qquad\partial_{n}\mathrm{H}^{\textnormal{ND}}_{S_{r}}(\cdot,z)|_{\mathbb{R}}=0.

Note that
\mathrm{H}^{\textnormal{ND}}_{S_{r}}(\cdot,\cdot)
is well-defined up to a multiplicative constant.
We choose the normalisation as

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathrm{H}^{\textnormal{ND}}_{S_{r}}(\cdot,x-\mathrm{i}\log r)\,\mathrm{d}x=1.

Then, we have the explicit formula

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{\textnormal{ND}}_{S_{r}}(x_{1},x_{2}-\mathrm{i}\log r)=\frac{1}{2|\log r|}\textnormal{sech}\Big(\frac{\pi\,(x_{2}-x_{1})}{2|\log r|}\Big).

As before, we fix
2n
distinct vertices
x_{1}^{\delta},\ldots,x_{n}^{\delta}\in\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega^{\delta}

and
v_{1}^{\delta},\ldots,v_{n}^{\delta}\in\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega^{\delta}
,
by convention labelled in counterclockwise order,
and we assume that as
\delta\to 0
, each
x^{\delta}_{j}
converges to
x_{j}\in\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega
and each
v^{\delta}_{j}
converges to
v_{j}\in\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega
,
where
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\in\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega
and
v_{1},\ldots,v_{n}\in\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega
are distinct.
(For the different copies in the universal over, we only use the subscript “
m
” trusting that it is clear from context.)
Choosing the branch of the argument function
\arg
to take values in
[-\pi,\pi)
, set

\displaystyle c:=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\arg\phi_{r}(x_{j})-\sum_{j=1}^{n}\arg\phi_{r}(e_{j}).

Define also

\displaystyle\Upsilon(\bm{x},\bm{v}):=\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq n}\sin\bigg(\frac{\arg\phi(x_{j})-\arg\phi(x_{i})}{2}\bigg)\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq n}\sin\bigg(\frac{\arg\phi(v_{j})-\arg\phi(v_{i})}{2}\bigg).

Lemma 2.11
.

Suppose
z^{\delta}\in\mathcal{V}^{\delta}
converge to
z\in\Omega\setminus\zeta
as
\delta\to 0
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to 0}\frac{\det\big(h^{\delta}_{2\pi\beta}(x^{\delta}_{i},v^{\delta}_{j})\big)_{1\leq i,j\leq n}}{\prod^{n}_{j=1}h^{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}(z^{\delta},v_{j}^{\delta})}=\frac{\det\big(h_{2\pi\beta}(x_{i},v_{j})\big)_{1\leq i,j\leq n}}{\prod^{n}_{j=1}\mathrm{H}^{\textnormal{ND}}_{S_{r}}\big((p^{-1}\circ\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}_{r})(z),\,(p^{-1}\circ\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}_{r})(v_{j})\big)}.

(2.36)

If
n
is odd, we have the following two cases as
r\to 0
.

1.

if
\beta\in[0,\frac{1}{2})\cup(\frac{1}{2},1)
,
and
N_{\beta}\in\mathbb{Z}
is such that
|\beta-N_{\beta}|=\underset{k\in\mathbb{Z}}{\min}\,|\beta-k|
, then we have

\displaystyle\det\big(h_{2\pi\beta}(x_{i},v_{j})\big)_{1\leq i,j\leq n}=\;

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi^{n}}\,\big(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}(\beta-N_{\beta})c}+o_{r}(1)\big)\;r^{\frac{n^{2}-1}{4}+|\beta-N_{\beta}|}\;\Upsilon(\bm{x},\bm{v});

(2.37)

2.

if
\beta=\frac{1}{2}
, then we have

\displaystyle\det\big(h_{2\pi\beta}(x_{i},v_{j})\big)_{1\leq i,j\leq n}=\;

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi^{n}}\,\big(2\cos(c/2)+o_{r}(1)\big)\;r^{\frac{n^{2}-1}{4}+\frac{1}{2}}\;\Upsilon(\bm{x},\bm{v}).

(2.38)

If
n
is even, and
N_{\beta}\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
|\beta-N_{\beta}-\frac{1}{2}|=\underset{k\in\mathbb{Z}}{\min}\,|\beta-k-\frac{1}{2}|
, then as
r\to 0
we have

\displaystyle\det\big(h_{2\pi\beta}(x_{i},v_{j})\big)_{1\leq i,j\leq n}=\;

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi^{n}}\,\big(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}(\beta-N_{\beta}+\frac{1}{2})c}+o_{r}(1)\big)\;r^{\frac{n^{2}}{4}}\;\Upsilon(\bm{x},\bm{v}).

(2.39)

To derive Lemma
2.11
, we will to deal with the corresponding quantity on the universal cover.
For
z\in\Omega
and
w\in\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega
, denoting by
w_{m}\in\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}
the
m
:th copy of
w=w_{0}
, define

\displaystyle h^{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}_{\beta}(z,w):=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}m\beta}\,\mathrm{H}^{\textnormal{ND}}_{S_{r}}\big((p^{-1}\circ\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}_{r})(z),\,(p^{-1}\circ\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}_{r})(w_{m})\big).

(2.40)

Lemma 2.12
.

Suppose
z^{\delta},x^{\delta}\in\mathcal{V}^{\delta}
, and
w^{\delta}\in\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\mathcal{V}^{\delta}
converge to
z,x\in\Omega\setminus\zeta
and
w=w_{0}\in\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega\setminus\zeta
as
\delta\to 0
.
Denote by
w_{m}^{\delta}\in\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\mathcal{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}^{\delta}
and
w_{m}\in\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}
the
m
:th copies of
w^{\delta}=w_{0}^{\delta}
and
w=w_{0}
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\frac{h^{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}(x^{\delta},w_{m}^{\delta})}{h^{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}(z^{\delta},w_{0}^{\delta})}=\frac{\mathrm{H}^{\textnormal{ND}}_{S_{r}}\big((p^{-1}\circ\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}_{r})(x),\,(p^{-1}\circ\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}_{r})(w_{m})\big)}{\mathrm{H}^{\textnormal{ND}}_{S_{r}}\big((p^{-1}\circ\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}_{r})(z),\,(p^{-1}\circ\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}_{r})(w_{0})\big)}.

(2.41)

In particular, for the function defined in (
2.2
), we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to 0}\frac{h^{\delta}_{\beta}(x^{\delta},w^{\delta})}{h^{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}(z^{\delta},w^{\delta})}=\frac{h^{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}_{\beta}(x,w)}{\mathrm{H}^{\textnormal{ND}}_{S_{r}}\big((p^{-1}\circ\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}_{r})(z),\,(p^{-1}\circ\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}_{r})(w)\big)}.

(2.42)

Proof.

The proof comprises standard discrete harmonic arguments.
Note that the right side of (
2.41
) is independent of the normalisation of the Poisson kernel.
Denote by
z_{m}^{\delta}\in\mathcal{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}^{\delta}
and
z_{m}\in\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}
the
m
:th copies of
z^{\delta}
and
z
.
Observe that

h^{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}(z_{0}^{\delta},w_{0}^{\delta})=h^{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}(z^{\delta}_{m},w^{\delta}_{m})

and the ratio
h^{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}(\cdot,w^{\delta}_{m})/h^{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}(z^{\delta}_{m},w^{\delta}_{m})

is discrete harmonic on
\mathcal{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}^{\delta}
, equalling
1
at
z^{\delta}_{m}
and
0
on
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\mathcal{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}^{\delta}\setminus\{w^{\delta}_{m}\}
.
By a discrete Harnack-type inequality, for every
\epsilon>0
, there are constants
A>0
and
\alpha>0
such that

\displaystyle\frac{h^{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}(u,w^{\delta}_{m})}{h^{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}(z^{\delta}_{m},w^{\delta}_{m})}\leq A\qquad\textnormal{and}\qquad\bigg|\frac{h^{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}(u,w^{\delta}_{m})}{h^{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}(z^{\delta}_{m},w^{\delta}_{m})}-\frac{h^{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}(v,w^{\delta}_{m})}{h^{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}(z^{\delta}_{m},w^{\delta}_{m})}\bigg|\leq A\,|u-v|^{a},\qquad u,v\in\mathcal{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}^{\delta}\setminus B(w^{\delta}_{m},\epsilon).

By the Arzela–Ascoli theorem, this (and the pointwise convergence on
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\mathcal{V}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}^{\delta}\setminus\{w^{\delta}_{m}\}
) imply the pre-compactness of

\big\{h^{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}(\cdot,w^{\delta}_{m})/h^{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}(z^{\delta}_{m},w^{\delta}_{m})\big\}_{\delta>0}
with respect to the uniform topology. Suppose
h
is any subsequential limit.
By the uniform convergence, we have
h=0
on
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}\setminus\{w_{m}\}
and
h(z_{m})=1
at

z_{m}=\lim_{\delta\to 0}z^{\delta}_{m}
.
By constructing the harmonic conjugate locally (e.g., see the proof of Proposition 4.2 in
[
LSW04
]
), we see that
\partial_{n}(h\circ(\phi^{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}_{r})^{-1})|_{\mathbb{R}}=0
.
Thus,
h(x)
equals the right-hand side of (
2.41
).

For (
2.42
), similarly to (
2.29
), there exists a constant
q\in(0,1)
and a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\frac{h^{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}(z^{\delta},w^{\delta}_{m})}{h^{\scalebox{0.6}{{univ}}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}(z^{\delta},w^{\delta})}\leq Cq^{|m|}.

Combining this with (
2.41
), we obtain (
2.42
).
∎

Proof of Lemma
2.11
.

The asserted Equation (
2.36
) was proved in Equation (
2.42
) in Lemma
2.12
.
The asymptotics (
2.37
) and (
2.39
) are given
by
[
AO19
, Proposition 5.5]
, which we recall briefly below.
By Equation (
2.40
) and
[
AO19
, Lemma A.2]
, we have

\displaystyle\det\big(h_{2\pi\beta}(x_{i},v_{j})\big)_{1\leq i,j\leq n}

(2.43)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{n}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1}<\cdots<k_{n}\\
k_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\det\big(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}(\beta-k_{i})\arg(\phi_{r}(x_{j}))}\big)_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\;\det\big(\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}(\beta-k_{i})\arg(\phi_{r}(v_{j}))}\big)_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\;\prod_{i=1}^{n}\textnormal{sech}\big(|\log r|(\beta-k_{i})\big).

We first consider the case of odd
n
.

•

If
\beta\in[0,\frac{1}{2})\cup(\frac{1}{2},1)
, the minimal power of
r
is obtained at

(k_{1},\ldots,k_{n})=(-\frac{n-1}{2}+N_{\beta},\ldots,\frac{n-1}{2}+N_{\beta})
.

[
AO19
, Proposition 5.5]
thus yields

\displaystyle\det\big(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}(\beta-k_{i})\arg(\phi_{r}(x_{j}))}\big)_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\;\det\big(\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}(\beta-k_{i})\arg(\phi_{r}(v_{j}))}\big)_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\;\prod_{i=1}^{n}\textnormal{sech}\big(|\log r|(\beta-k_{i})\big)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\big(2^{n}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}(\beta-N_{\beta})c}+o_{r}(1)\big)\;r^{\frac{n^{2}-1}{4}+|\beta-N_{\beta}|}\;\Upsilon(\bm{x},\bm{v}),\qquad r\to 0.

By Lemma
2.13
given below, there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\big|\det\big(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}(\beta-k_{i})\arg(\phi_{r}(x_{j}))}\big)_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\big|\;\big|\det\big(\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}(\beta-k_{i})\arg(\phi_{r}(v_{j}))}\big)_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\big|\;\prod_{i=1}^{n}\textnormal{sech}\big(|\log r|(\beta-k_{i})\big)

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle C\;r^{\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\beta-k_{i}|}\;\prod_{i=1}^{n}(|k_{i}|+1)^{n}\;\Upsilon(\bm{x},\bm{v}),\qquad r\to 0.

Plugging this into (
2.43
), we obtain (
2.37
).

•

If
\beta=\frac{1}{2}
, the minimal power of
r
is obtained at

(k_{1},\ldots,k_{n})=(-\frac{n-1}{2}+\beta-\frac{1}{2},\ldots,\frac{n-1}{2}+\beta-\frac{1}{2})
,
or
(k_{1},\ldots,k_{n})=(-\frac{n-1}{2}+\beta+\frac{1}{2},\ldots,\frac{n-1}{2}+\beta+\frac{1}{2})
.
Equivalently,
\beta-\frac{1}{2}
and
\beta+\frac{1}{2}
are both integers closest to
\beta
.
By a similar computation as above in the proof of (
2.37
), we obtain (
2.38
).

Next, we consider the case of even
n
.
In this case, the minimal power of
r
is obtained at
(k_{1},\ldots,k_{n})=(-\frac{n}{2}+N_{\beta}+1,\ldots,\frac{n}{2}+N_{\beta})
, and
[
AO19
, Proposition 5.5]
yields

\displaystyle\det\big(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}(\beta-k_{i})\arg(\phi(x_{j}))}\big)_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\;\det\big(\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}(\beta-k_{i})\arg(\phi(v_{j}))}\big)_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\;\prod_{i=1}^{n}\textnormal{sech}\big(|\log r|(\beta-k_{i})\big)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\big(2^{n}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}(\beta-N_{\beta}-\frac{1}{2})c}+o_{r}(1)\big)\;r^{\frac{n^{2}}{4}}\;\Upsilon(\bm{x},\bm{v}),\qquad r\to 0.

By a similar argument as in the proof of (
2.37
), we obtain (
2.39
). This completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.1
.

This is a collection of Proposition
2.3
and Lemmas
2.10
and
2.11
.
∎

Lemma 2.13
.

Suppose
f_{1},\ldots f_{n}
are analytic functions on
\partial\mathbb{D}
, and let
f_{i}^{(j)}
denote the
j
-th derivative of
f_{i}
.
Then, there exists a constant
C>0
, which only depends on
n
, such that

\displaystyle\big|\det\big(f_{i}(z_{j})\big)_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\big|\leq C\,\Big(\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq n}|z_{j}-z_{i}|\Big)\bigg(\prod^{n}_{i=1}\max_{1\leq j\leq n}\max_{z\in\partial\mathbb{D}}|f^{(j-1)}_{i}(z)|\bigg).

(2.44)

Proof.

Writing

\displaystyle\det\big(f_{i}(z_{j})\big)_{1\leq i,j\leq n}=\;

\displaystyle\Big(\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq n}(z_{j}-z_{i})\Big)\;\det\big(F_{i,j}(z_{j})\big)_{1\leq i,j\leq n},

\displaystyle F_{i,1}(w):=f_{i}(w),\qquad F_{i,j}(w):=\;

\displaystyle\frac{F_{i,j-1}(w)-F_{i,j-1}(z_{j-1})}{w-z_{j-1}},\qquad i\in\{1,\ldots,n\},\;j\in\{2,\ldots,n\},

we inductively find that

\displaystyle F_{i,j}(z_{j})=\int_{0}^{1}\lambda_{1}^{j-1}\mathrm{d}\lambda_{1}\int_{0}^{1}\lambda_{2}^{j-2}\mathrm{d}\lambda_{2}\cdots\int^{1}_{0}\mathrm{d}\lambda_{j-1}\;f_{i}^{(j-1)}\bigg(\sum^{j-1}_{k=1}\lambda_{1}\cdots\lambda_{k-1}(1-\lambda_{k})z_{k}\,+\,\lambda_{1}\cdots\lambda_{j-1}z_{j}\bigg),

and expanding the determinant yields (
2.44
).
∎

3
Scaling limit of LERW rays: proof of Theorem
1.2

In this section, we will use the same setup and notation as in Sections
1
–
2
.
Our aim is to establish the convergence of the hitting points of the UST branches at the outer boundary (Proposition
3.1
in Section
3.1
), and the convergence of UST branches themselves (Proposition
3.4
in Section
3.2
).
We finish with the proof of Theorem
1.2
in the end of Section
3.2
.
Knowing the scaling limit of the curves, we compute their winding variance in Section
3.3
(yielding Proposition
1.4
).

3.1
Scaling limit of the law of hitting points

Next, we derive the scaling limit of the conditional law of the hitting points of the UST branches,

\bm{\gamma}^{\delta}(\bm{\tau}^{\delta})=(\gamma_{1}^{\delta}(\tau_{1}^{\delta}),\ldots,\gamma_{n}^{\delta}(\tau_{n}^{\delta}))
, to the outer boundary
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega^{\delta}

on the event
E^{\delta}_{\bm{x}}
, by first letting
\delta\to 0
and then shrinking
\diam(\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega)\to 0
.
Let
\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{n}
be
n
disjoint arcs located on
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega
in counterclockwise order, and suppose that

\alpha^{\delta}_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{n}^{\delta}
are
n
disjoint arcs on
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega^{\delta}
such that

\dist_{X}(\alpha_{j}^{\delta},\alpha_{j})\to 0
as
\delta\to 0
.
Consider the event

\displaystyle E^{\delta}_{\bm{x},\bm{\alpha},k}:=\{\vec{\gamma}^{\delta}_{j}\textnormal{ hits }\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega^{\delta}\textnormal{ at }\alpha_{j+k}^{\delta},\textnormal{ for each }1\leq j\leq n\},\qquad 1\leq k\leq n,

where we use the convention that
\alpha^{\delta}_{j+k}=\alpha^{\delta}_{j+k-n}
if
j+k>n
.
Define also the event

\displaystyle E^{\delta}_{\bm{x},\bm{\alpha}}=E(\Omega^{\delta};x_{1}^{\delta},\ldots,x_{n}^{\delta};\alpha_{1}^{\delta},\ldots,\alpha_{n}^{\delta}):=\bigcup_{k=0}^{n-1}E^{\delta}_{\bm{x},\bm{\alpha},k}.

(3.1)

Recall that we denote by
\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}}}
the simply connected domain bounded by
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega
, and we let

\phi\colon\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}}}\to\mathbb{D}
be the conformal bijection sending it onto the disc
\mathbb{D}=\{z\in\mathbb{C}\,|\,|z|<1\}

such that
\phi(0)=0
and
\phi^{\prime}(0)>0
.
Let
\phi_{r}\colon\Omega\to\mathbb{A}_{r}
be the conformal isomorphism onto the annulus

\mathbb{A}_{r}=A(r,1)
, so that
\phi_{r}
coincides with the restriction to
\Omega
of the conformal isomorphism
\phi\colon\Omega\to\mathbb{D}
.

In the odd case, Proposition
3.1
gives the asymptotics as
r\to 0
claimed in Theorem
1.3
.
Observe, however, that in the even case, the Brownian loop soup term depends on
r
, because
\Omega
does.
Therefore, we cannot directly infer the asymptotics as
r\to 0
from Proposition
3.1
in the even case.

Proposition 3.1
.

For every
n\geq 1
, the following scaling limit holds.
When
n
is odd, as
r\to 0
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to 0}\mathbb{P}^{\delta}[E^{\delta}_{\bm{x},\bm{\alpha}}]=\;

\displaystyle\frac{r^{\frac{n^{2}-1}{4}}}{\pi^{n}}\,\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq n}\sin\bigg(\frac{\arg\phi(x_{j})-\arg\phi(x_{i})}{2}\bigg)

(3.2)

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\mathbb{1}{\{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\bm{\theta}}\in\mathcal{X}_{n}\}}\mathbb{1}{\{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\theta_{j}}\in\phi(\alpha_{j}),\,\forall~j\}}\prod_{1\leq j<k\leq n}\sin\Big(\frac{\theta_{j}-\theta_{k}}{2}\Big)\prod_{j=1}^{n}\mathrm{d}\theta_{j}\;+\;o\big(r^{\frac{n^{2}-1}{4}}\big).

When
n
is even, as
r\to 0
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to 0}\mathbb{P}^{\delta}[E^{\delta}_{\bm{x},\bm{\alpha}}]=\;

\displaystyle\exp\Big(\!-2\mu_{\Omega}^{\textnormal{ND}}[\{\ell\in\mathcal{L}_{*}\,|\,\tfrac{\varphi(\ell)}{2\pi}\textnormal{ is odd }\}]\Big)\Bigg(\frac{r^{\frac{n^{2}}{4}}}{\pi^{n}}\,\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq n}\sin\bigg(\frac{\arg\phi(x_{j})-\arg\phi(x_{i})}{2}\bigg)

(3.3)

\displaystyle\quad\times\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\mathbb{1}{\{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\bm{\theta}}\in\mathcal{X}_{n}\}}\mathbb{1}{\{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\theta_{j}}\in\phi(\alpha_{j}),\,\forall~j\}}\prod_{1\leq j<k\leq n}\sin\Big(\frac{\theta_{j}-\theta_{k}}{2}\Big)\prod_{j=1}^{n}\mathrm{d}\theta_{j}\;+\;o\big(r^{\frac{n^{2}-1}{4}}\big)\Bigg).

Moreover, for every
n\geq 1
, the following scaling limit holds:

\displaystyle\lim_{r\to 0}\lim_{\delta\to 0}\frac{\mathbb{P}^{\delta}[E^{\delta}_{\bm{x},\bm{\alpha}}]}{\mathbb{P}^{\delta}[E^{\delta}_{\bm{x}}]}=\;

\displaystyle\frac{2^{\frac{n(n-1)}{2}}}{\mathcal{Z}_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\mathbb{1}{\{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\bm{\theta}}\in\mathcal{X}_{n}\}}\mathbb{1}{\{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\theta_{j}}\in\phi(\alpha_{j}),\,\forall~j\}}\prod_{1\leq j<k\leq n}\sin\Big(\frac{\theta_{j}-\theta_{k}}{2}\Big)\prod_{j=1}^{n}\mathrm{d}\theta_{j}

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathcal{Z}_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\mathbb{1}{\{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\bm{\theta}}\in\mathcal{X}_{n}\}}\mathbb{1}{\{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\theta_{j}}\in\phi(\alpha_{j}),\,\forall~j\}}\prod_{1\leq j<k\leq n}\big|\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\theta_{j}}-\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\theta_{k}}\big|\prod_{j=1}^{n}\mathrm{d}\theta_{j},

(3.4)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{n}:=\;

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\mathbb{1}{\{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\bm{\theta}}\in\mathcal{X}_{n}\}}\prod_{1\leq j<k\leq n}\big|\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\theta_{j}}-\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\theta_{k}}\big|\prod_{j=1}^{n}\mathrm{d}\theta_{j}

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle 2^{\frac{n(n-1)}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\mathbb{1}{\{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\bm{\theta}}\in\mathcal{X}_{n}\}}\prod_{1\leq j<k\leq n}\sin\Big(\frac{\theta_{j}-\theta_{k}}{2}\Big)\prod_{j=1}^{n}\mathrm{d}\theta_{j}.

In particular, the conditional law of
\bm{\gamma}^{\delta}(\bm{\tau}^{\delta})
given
E^{\delta}_{\bm{x}}
converges weakly when first letting
\delta\to 0
and then shrinking
\diam(\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega)\to 0
.
The density of the limit points
\bm{y}=(y_{1},\ldots,y_{n})
on
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega^{n}
is obtained from (
1.8
) by conformal invariance;
the density of
\phi(\bm{y})=(\phi(y_{1}),\ldots,\phi(y_{n}))
on
\mathbb{S}^{n}
equals
\rho(\phi(\bm{y}))
in (
1.8
).

Proof.

n
odd.
We first consider the case where
n
is odd.
For a boundary arc
\alpha\subset\partial B(0,1)
, denote by
h_{\smash{\mathbb{A}_{r}}}(\cdot,\alpha)

the bounded harmonic function on
\smash{\mathbb{A}_{r}}
with mixed Dirichlet-Neumann boundary conditions:

\displaystyle h_{\smash{\mathbb{A}_{r}}}(\cdot,\alpha)|_{\partial B(0,1)\setminus\alpha}=0,\qquad h_{\smash{\mathbb{A}_{r}}}(\cdot,\alpha)|_{\alpha}=1,\qquad\textnormal{and}\qquad\partial_{n}h_{\smash{\mathbb{A}_{r}}}(\cdot,\alpha)|_{\partial B(0,r)}=0.

After taking the sum over the edges along
\alpha^{\delta}_{j}
for
j=1,\ldots,n
, combining (
2.10
) from Lemma
2.4

and (
2.37
) from Lemma
2.11
,
the asserted Equation (
3.2
) follows from the exact determinantal identity

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to 0}\mathbb{P}^{\delta}[E^{\delta}_{\bm{x},\bm{\alpha}}]=\;

\displaystyle\det\big(h_{\mathbb{A}_{r}}(\phi(x_{i}),\phi(\alpha_{j}))\big)_{1\leq i,j\leq n}.

(3.5)

For the denominator
\mathbb{P}^{\delta}[E^{\delta}_{\bm{x}}]
, take a large
m\in\mathbb{N}
and set

\xi_{j,m}:=\phi^{-1}\big(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{2\pi j}{m}},\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\frac{2\pi(j+1)}{m}}\big)
,
and let
\xi_{j,m}^{\delta}
be its discrete approximation on
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega^{\delta}
, for
0\leq j\leq m-1
.
Write
\bm{\xi}=(\xi_{1,m}^{\delta},\ldots,\xi_{m,m}^{\delta})
.
By (
3.5
), we find

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to 0}\sum_{0\leq m_{1}\cdots<m_{n}\leq m-1}\mathbb{P}^{\delta}[E^{\delta}_{\bm{x},\bm{\xi}}]=\;

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq m_{1}\cdots<m_{n}\leq m-1}\det\big(h_{\mathbb{A}_{r}}(\phi(x_{i}),\phi(\xi_{m_{j},m}))\big)_{1\leq i,j\leq n}.

(3.6)

Consider now the event

\displaystyle A^{\delta}_{\bm{\xi}}:=\big\{\textnormal{there exist at least two hitting points of }\{\gamma_{1}^{\delta},\ldots,\gamma^{\delta}_{n}\}\textnormal{ in }\xi^{\delta}_{j,m}\textnormal{ for some }0\leq j\leq m-1\big\}.

Then, up to a constant independent of
n
and
m
, we have

\displaystyle\limsup_{\delta\to 0}\mathbb{P}^{\delta}[A^{\delta}_{\bm{\xi}}]\leq\;

\displaystyle n^{2}\,\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\Big(h_{\mathbb{A}_{r}}(\phi(x_{i}),\phi(\xi_{j,m}))\Big)^{2}\lesssim\frac{n^{2}}{m}.

(3.7)

Combining (
3.2
,
3.6
,
3.7
) together, we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to 0}\mathbb{P}^{\delta}[E^{\delta}_{\bm{x}}]=\;

\displaystyle\lim_{m\to\infty}\sum_{0\leq m_{1}\cdots<m_{n}\leq m-1}\det\big(h_{\mathbb{A}_{r}}(\phi(x_{i}),\phi(\xi_{m_{j},m}))\big)_{1\leq i,j\leq n}

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\frac{r^{\frac{n^{2}-1}{4}}}{\pi^{n}}\,\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq n}\sin\bigg(\frac{\arg\phi(x_{j})-\arg\phi(x_{i})}{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\mathbb{1}{\{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\bm{\theta}}\in\mathcal{X}_{n}\}}\mathbb{1}{\{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\theta_{j}}\in\phi(\alpha_{j}),\,\forall~j\}}\prod_{1\leq j<k\leq n}\sin\Big(\frac{\theta_{j}-\theta_{k}}{2}\Big)\prod_{j=1}^{n}\mathrm{d}\theta_{j}\;+\;o\big(r^{\frac{n^{2}-1}{4}}\big).

For odd
n
, the asserted Equation (
3.4
) now follows combining with the limit (
3.5
).

Now suppose that
n
is even.
The proof is similar.
Taking the sum over the edges along
\alpha^{\delta}_{j}
for
j=1,\ldots,n
, combining (
2.14
) from Lemma
2.5

and (
2.39
) from Lemma
2.11
with Lemma
2.10
,
the asserted Equation (
3.3
) follows from

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to 0}\mathbb{P}^{\delta}[E^{\delta}_{\bm{x},\bm{\alpha}}]=\;

\displaystyle\exp\Big(\!-2\mu_{\Omega}^{\textnormal{ND}}[\{\ell\in\mathcal{L}_{*}\,|\,\tfrac{\varphi(\ell)}{2\pi}\textnormal{ is odd }\}]\Big)\;\det\big(h_{\mathbb{A}_{r}}(\phi(x_{i}),\phi(\alpha_{j}))\big)_{1\leq i,j\leq n}.

The argument to obtain the asserted Equation (
3.4
) is the same as in the odd case.
∎

We now establish a relation between Brownian motion with mixed boundary conditions and Brownian motion with Dirichlet boundary conditions.
Recall that we denote by
\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}}}
the domain bounded by
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega
.

Proposition 3.2
.

Suppose
\varrho
is a simple curve on
\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}}}
which only intersects
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega
at its starting point and does not hit
0
. Then, for every
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\diam(\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega)\to 0}\mu^{\textnormal{ND}}_{\Omega}\big[\mathbb{1}{\{\ell\in\mathcal{L}_{*}\textnormal{ and }\ell\cap\varrho\neq\emptyset\}}\;\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\beta\varphi(\ell)}\big]=\mu_{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}}}}\big[\mathbb{1}{\{\ell\in\mathcal{L}_{*}\textnormal{ and }\ell\cap\varrho\neq\emptyset\}}\;\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\beta\varphi(\ell)}\big].

(3.8)

In particular, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\diam(\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega)\to 0}\exp\Big(2\mu_{\Omega}^{\textnormal{ND}}\big[\ell\in\mathcal{L}_{*}\textnormal{ such that }\tfrac{1}{2\pi}\varphi(\ell)\textnormal{ is odd and }\ell\cap\varrho\neq\emptyset\big]\Big)=\bigg|\frac{\phi_{\varrho}^{\prime}(0)}{\phi^{\prime}(0)}\bigg|^{1/4},

(3.9)

where
\phi\colon\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}}}\to\mathbb{D}
and
\phi_{\varrho}\colon\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}}}\setminus\varrho\to\mathbb{D}
are any conformal isomorphisms fixing the origin.

Proof.

We take
\Omega^{\delta}=\Omega\cap\delta\mathbb{Z}^{2}
and take
\varrho^{\delta}
to be an approximation of
\varrho
such that
\dist(\varrho^{\delta},\varrho)\leq 2\delta
.
Rescaling the loops on
\mathbb{Z}^{2}
as described Section
2.1

and denoting by
\Lambda^{\textnormal{D}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}
the restriction of the random walk loop measure on loops contained in
\Omega^{\delta}\setminus\partial\Omega^{\delta}
, from the definitions we obtain

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\textnormal{ND}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}[\,\cdot\,\mathbb{1}{\{\ell\textnormal{ is contained in }\Omega^{\delta}\setminus\partial\Omega^{\delta}\}}]=\Lambda^{\textnormal{D}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}[\,\cdot\,].

In particular, we have

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\textnormal{ND}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}\big[\mathbb{1}{\{\ell\in\mathcal{L}_{*}^{\delta}\textnormal{ and }\ell\cap\varrho^{\delta}\neq\emptyset\}}\,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\beta\varphi(\ell)}\big]

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\textnormal{D}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}\big[\mathbb{1}{\{\ell\in\mathcal{L}_{*}^{\delta}\textnormal{ and }\ell\cap\varrho^{\delta}\neq\emptyset\}}\,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\beta\varphi(\ell)}\big]\;+\;\Lambda^{\textnormal{ND}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}\big[\mathbb{1}{\{\ell\in\mathcal{L}_{*}^{\delta}\textnormal{ and }\ell\cap\varrho^{\delta}\neq\emptyset,\textnormal{ and }\ell\cap\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega^{\delta}\neq\emptyset\}}\,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\beta\varphi(\ell)}\big].

For the first term, by
[
LF04
, Theorem 1.1]
we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to 0}\Lambda^{\textnormal{D}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}\big[\mathbb{1}{\{\ell\in\mathcal{L}_{*}^{\delta}\textnormal{ and }\ell\cap\varrho^{\delta}\neq\emptyset\}}\,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\beta\varphi(\ell)}\big]=\mu_{\Omega}\big[\mathbb{1}{\{\ell\in\mathcal{L}_{*}\textnormal{ and }\ell\cap\varrho\neq\emptyset\}}\,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\beta\varphi(\ell)}\big].

(3.10)

Consider then the second term.
Let
d=\frac{1}{4}\dist(\varrho,0)
and choose
\epsilon_{0}\in(0,\frac{d}{100})
and
\delta_{0}^{\prime}\in(0,\frac{1}{4}d)
such that
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega\cup\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega^{\delta}\subset B(0,\epsilon_{0})
for all
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0}^{\prime})
.
Note that any loop on
\Omega^{\delta}
which intersects both
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega^{\delta}
and
\varrho^{\delta}
cannot be contained in
\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(d)}
, and we must have

\diam(\ell)\geq d/2
.
Fix
\varepsilon>0
. By (
2.30
) and Lemma
2.8
,
we can choose
M=M(\varepsilon,d)>1
large enough and
\delta_{0}=\delta_{0}(\varepsilon,d)>0
small enough such that

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{ND}}\big[\ell\not\subset\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(d)},\,\diam(\ell)\geq d/2,\textnormal{ and its lifetime }t_{\ell}\notin\big[\tfrac{1}{M},M\big]\big]\leq\;

\displaystyle\varepsilon,\qquad\textnormal{ for all }\delta\in(0,\delta_{0}).

By (
2.3
) and Lemma
2.8
,
we can furthermore choose
c=c(\varepsilon,d)>0
small enough such that

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{ND}}\big[\ell\not\subset\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(d)},\,\diam(\ell)\geq d/2,\textnormal{ its lifetime }t_{\ell}\in\big(\tfrac{1}{M},M\big),\textnormal{ and its root lies in }\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c)}\big]\leq\;

\displaystyle\varepsilon,

for all
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0})
.
Now, let
\tau_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}
be the hitting time of the random walk
\mathcal{R}
at
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega^{\delta}
. As before, we have

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\Omega^{\delta}}^{\textnormal{ND}}\big[\ell\in\mathcal{L}_{*}^{\delta},\textnormal{ its lifetime }t_{\ell}\in\big(\tfrac{1}{M},M\big),\textnormal{ its root lies not in }\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c)},\textnormal{ and }\ell\cap\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega^{\delta}\neq\emptyset\big]

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}z\in\mathcal{V}^{\delta}\setminus\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\mathcal{V}^{\delta}\\
z\not\in\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c)}\end{subarray}}\frac{\delta^{2}}{2}\sum_{t\in\frac{1}{2}\delta^{2}\mathbb{N}\cap[\frac{1}{M},M]}\frac{1}{t}\,\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{z}\big[\mathcal{R}[0,t]\cap\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega^{\delta}\neq\emptyset\textnormal{ and }\mathcal{R}(t)=z\big]

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}z\in\mathcal{V}^{\delta}\setminus\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\mathcal{V}^{\delta}\\
z\not\in\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c)}\end{subarray}}\frac{\delta^{2}}{2}\,M\,\mathsf{E}^{\delta}_{z}\bigg[\mathbb{1}{\{\tau_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}<\infty\}}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}})}[\mathcal{R}(j)=z]\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\frac{M}{2}\Leb(\tilde{\Omega}^{\delta})\;\Big(\max_{z\not\in\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c)}}\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{z}[\tau_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}<\infty]\Big)\;\underbrace{\Big(\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}z\notin\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c)}\\
|w-z|\geq c\end{subarray}}\mathrm{G}^{\textnormal{ND}}_{\Omega^{\delta}}(w,z)\Big)}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\leq\;C(c)\;\textnormal{by Lemma~\ref{lem::Green_mix}}\\
\textnormal{whenever $\delta$ is small enough}\end{subarray}}

(3.11)

Taking
\delta\to 0
, we obtain
\Leb(\tilde{\Omega}^{\delta})\to\Leb(\Omega)
and

\displaystyle\limsup_{\delta\to 0}\max_{z\not\in\mathcal{V}^{\delta}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}{(c)}}\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{z}[\tau_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}<\infty]\leq\frac{\log c}{\log\diam(\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega)}.

(3.12)

Combining (
3.10
,
3.11
,
3.12
) together,
by first letting
\delta\to 0
, then letting
\diam(\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega)\to 0
and finally letting
\varepsilon\to 0
, we complete the proof of (
3.8
).

For (
3.9
), note that by (
3.8
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lim_{\diam(\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega)\to 0}\;&\mu_{\Omega}^{\textnormal{ND}}\big[\ell\in\mathcal{L}_{*}\textnormal{ such that }\tfrac{1}{2\pi}\varphi(\ell)\textnormal{ is odd and }\ell\cap\varrho\neq\emptyset\big]\\
=\;&\mu_{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}}}}\big[\ell\in\mathcal{L}_{*}\textnormal{ such that }\tfrac{1}{2\pi}\varphi(\ell)\textnormal{ is odd and }\ell\cap\varrho\neq\emptyset\big].\end{split}

(3.13)

Combining this with an argument similar to
[
BCL18
, Proof of Proposition 4.3]
, we are able to complete the proof.
Indeed, using the restriction covariance property
[
Wer08
, Proposition 3]

combined with
[
GF06
, Theorems 5.1 & 5.2]

(which rely on a direct computation and a result of Yor
[
Yor80
]
for the law of the index of a Brownian loop around a fixed point),
we can write

\displaystyle\textnormal{\eqref{eqn::conv_loop_odd_aux}}=\;

\displaystyle\sum_{k=-\infty}^{\infty}\mu_{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}}}}\big[\ell\in\mathcal{L}_{*}\textnormal{ such that }\tfrac{1}{2\pi}\varphi(\ell)=2k+1\textnormal{ and }\ell\cap\varrho\neq\emptyset\big]

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\sum_{k=-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2\pi^{2}(2k+1)^{2}}\,\log\bigg|\frac{\phi_{\varrho}^{\prime}(0)}{\phi^{\prime}(0)}\bigg|\;=\;\frac{1}{8}\,\log\bigg|\frac{\phi_{\varrho}^{\prime}(0)}{\phi^{\prime}(0)}\bigg|,

which yields (
3.9
).
∎

In Lemma
A.1
, we will provide a self-contained proof of (
3.9
) via a martingale technique.

3.2
Scaling limit of LERW rays: proof of Theorem
1.2

We now consider the collection
\bm{\eta}=(\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{n})
of
n
random curves in the unit disc
\mathbb{D}
as in Section
1.3
.
To describe the scaling limit of the UST branches
\bm{\gamma}^{\delta}=(\gamma_{1}^{\delta},\ldots,\gamma_{n}^{\delta})
,
it is convenient to consider their time-reversals
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\gamma}$}}$}{}^{\delta}=(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{1}^{\delta},\ldots,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{n}^{\delta})
,
reparametrised by common capacity time to obtain curves
\bm{\eta}^{\delta}=(\eta_{1}^{\delta},\ldots,\eta_{n}^{\delta})
such that

-\log\CR(0;\mathbb{D}\setminus\bm{\eta}^{\delta}[0,t])=nt
,
where the conformal radius
\CR(0;\mathbb{D}\setminus\bm{\eta}^{\delta}[0,t])=1/g^{\prime}(0)
is defined by the distortion
g^{\prime}(0)

of the conformal isomorphism
g\colon\mathbb{D}\setminus\bm{\eta}^{\delta}[0,t]\to\mathbb{D}
such that
g(0)=0
and
g^{\prime}(0)>0
.

To begin, we will prove the convergence of
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\gamma}$}}$}{}^{\delta}
, by first letting
\delta\to 0
and then
\diam(\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega)\to 0
(Proposition
3.4
).
Then, we prove that after reparametrisation, the limiting curve has the same law as
\bm{\eta}
(Lemma
3.7
),
with starting point density (
1.8
) and Loewner driving functions given by Dyson Brownian motion (
1.9
)
— that is, a
n
-sided radial
\SLE_{2}
process.
Let us first briefly recall the definition of
n
-sided radial
\SLE_{\kappa}
(see
[
HL21
]
for more details)
and
\SLE_{\kappa}
with force points (see
[
SW05
]
for more details).

Consider
n
simple curves
\bm{\varpi}=(\varpi_{1},\ldots,\varpi_{n})
in
\mathbb{D}
such that each
\varpi_{j}
intersects
\partial\mathbb{D}
only at its starting point, and they are disjoint before reaching
0
.
They can be described by the
radial Loewner flow

g_{t}
(i.e., the conformal isomorphism
g_{t}\colon\mathbb{D}\setminus\bm{\varpi}[0,t]\to\mathbb{D}
such that
g_{t}(0)=0
and
g_{t}^{\prime}(0)>0
), which satisfies the flowing ODE

\displaystyle\partial_{t}g_{t}(z)=g_{t}(z)\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta_{j}(t)}+g_{t}(z)}{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta_{j}(t)}-g_{t}(z)},\qquad t\geq 0,\qquad\textnormal{where }\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta_{j}(t)}=g_{t}(\varpi_{j}(t)).

(3.14)

Here and throughout, the time-parametrisation of the curves is the common parametrisation (by capacity).
If there is just one curve, we parametrise it by default by its capacity.
For
\kappa\in(0,4]
, the
n
-sided radial
\SLE_{\kappa}
process is obtained by taking the Loewner driving functions to be the unique strong solution
\bm{\Theta}(t)=(\Theta_{1}(t),\ldots,\Theta_{n}(t))
to the SDEs
9
9

9

We emphasize that the time
t
in the present article corresponds to time
\frac{\kappa}{4}t
in
[
HL21
]
,
and
\Theta_{j}(t)
corresponds to
2\theta_{t}^{j}
in
[
HL21
]
.
The choice of our normalisation is more suitable in the setting of flow-line couplings/imaginary geometry.

(compare with (
1.9
))

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\Theta_{i}(t)=\sqrt{\kappa}\,\mathrm{d}W_{i}(t)+2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq n\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}\cot\bigg(\frac{\Theta_{i}(t)-\Theta_{j}(t)}{2}\bigg)\mathrm{d}t,\qquad 1\leq i\leq n,

(3.15)

where
(W_{i}(t)\colon t\geq 0)
for
1\leq i\leq n
are
n
independent Brownian motions on
\mathbb{R}
with
W_{i}(0)=\theta_{i}
for each
i
.

We denote the
n
-sided radial
\SLE_{\kappa}
curves in common parametrisation by
\bm{\eta}(t)=(\eta_{1}(t),\ldots,\eta_{n}(t))
.
Up to any time before the curves meet, its law (for
\kappa\in(0,4]
) is absolutely continuous with respect to that of
n
independent radial
\SLE_{\kappa}
curves in the common parametrisation,
which we shall denote by
\bm{\varpi}(t)=(\varpi_{1}(t),\ldots,\varpi_{n}(t))
throughout; see
[
HL21
, Theorem 3.12]
.

We will also use
\SLE_{\kappa}
curves with force points.
For
p\geq 2
and
\bm{\rho}:=(\rho_{2},\ldots,\rho_{p})\in\mathbb{R}^{p-1}
, we define radial
\SLE_{\kappa}(\bm{\rho})

in
(\mathbb{D};\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\bm{\theta}};0)
with
p
distinct boundary force points
(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\theta_{2}},\ldots,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\theta_{p}})\in\mathbb{S}^{p-1}

as the curve
\varpi
started at
\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\theta_{1}}\in\mathbb{S}
whose Loewner driving function
\Theta
solves the SDEs

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\Theta(t)=\;

\displaystyle\sqrt{\kappa}\,\mathrm{d}W(t)-\sum_{j=2}^{p}\frac{\rho_{j}}{2}\cot\bigg(\frac{V_{j}(t)-\Theta(t)}{2}\bigg)\mathrm{d}t,\qquad\Theta(0)=\theta_{1},

(3.16)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}V_{j}(t)=\;

\displaystyle\cot\bigg(\frac{V_{j}(t)-\Theta(t)}{2}\bigg)\mathrm{d}t,\qquad V_{j}(0)=\theta_{j},\qquad j\in\{2,\ldots,p\},

(3.17)

where
\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}V_{j}(t)}=g_{t}(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\theta_{j}})
are the time-evolutions of the force points, and
W
is a one-dimensional Brownian motion;
the associated Loewner flow
g_{t}\colon\mathbb{D}\setminus\varpi[0,t]\to\mathbb{D}
(with
g_{t}(0)=0
and
g_{t}^{\prime}(0)>0
) satisfies the ODE

\displaystyle\partial_{t}g_{t}(z)=g_{t}(z)\,\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}+g_{t}(z)}{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}-g_{t}(z)},\qquad t\geq 0.

(3.18)

We define radial
\SLE_{\kappa}(\bm{\rho})
in
\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}}}
started at
z_{1}\in\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega
with force points
z_{2},\ldots,z_{n}\in\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega
(all distinct)
as the pushforward measure of
\SLE_{\kappa}(\bm{\rho})
from
(\mathbb{D};\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\bm{\theta}};0)

by the conformal bijection
\phi^{-1}\colon\mathbb{D}\to\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}}}
, where
\phi(z_{j})=:\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\theta_{j}}
, for each
j
.
We will be interested in the case where
\rho_{j}=2
for all
j
.

Now, let
\bm{z}=(z_{1},\ldots,z_{n})
be
n
distinct points located on
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega
in counterclockwise order, and let
\kappa\in(0,4]
.
(Later, we will set
\kappa=2
.)

Definition 3.3
.

For
\epsilon>0
, we sample random curves
(\eta^{\bm{z}}_{1,\epsilon},\ldots,\eta^{\bm{z}}_{n,\epsilon})
as follows.

•

The law of
\eta^{\bm{z}}_{1,\epsilon}
is that of radial
\SLE_{\kappa}(2,\ldots,2)
from
z_{1}
to
0
on
\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}}}

stopped when hitting
\partial B(0,\epsilon)
, with boundary force points
z_{2},\ldots,z_{n}
.
We let
T_{1,\epsilon}
be a stopping time for
\eta^{\bm{z}}_{1,\epsilon}
before the hitting time.

•

Inductively, for
2\leq j\leq n
, the conditional law of
\eta^{\bm{z}}_{j,\epsilon}
given
(\eta^{\bm{z}}_{1,\epsilon},\ldots,\eta^{\bm{z}}_{j-1,\epsilon})

is that of the law of radial
\SLE_{\kappa}(2,\ldots,2)
from
z_{j}
to
0
on
\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}}}\setminus\cup_{i=1}^{j-1}\eta^{\bm{z}}_{i,\epsilon}
stopped when hitting
\partial B(0,\epsilon)
,
with force points
z_{j+1},\ldots,z_{n},\eta^{\bm{z}}_{1,\epsilon}(T_{1,\epsilon}),\ldots,\eta^{\bm{z}}_{j-1,\epsilon}(T_{j-1,\epsilon})
.
We let
T_{j,\epsilon}
be a stopping time for
\eta^{\bm{z}}_{j,\epsilon}
before the hitting time.

We call this the
inductive construction
in
\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}}}
from
\bm{z}
to
0
.

The next result gives the key scaling limit description for the LERW rays.
The convergence takes place weakly for probability measures on the complete and separable metric space
(X,\dist_{X})
of planar oriented unparametrised curves with the standard metric

\displaystyle\dist_{X}(\eta,\tilde{\eta}):=\inf_{\varrho,\tilde{\varrho}}\sup_{t\in[0,1]}|\eta(\varrho(t))-\tilde{\eta}(\tilde{\varrho}(t))|,\qquad\eta,\tilde{\eta}\in X,

(3.19)

where the infimum is taken over all increasing homeomorphisms
\varrho,\tilde{\varrho}\colon[0,1]\to[0,1]
.
Abusing notation, we also write
||\,\eta-\tilde{\eta}\,||_{\rm TV}
for the total variation distance between the laws of two random variables
\eta,\tilde{\eta}
in
X
.
For many curves, we use the metric space
X_{0}^{n}=X_{0}^{n}(\Omega)\subset X^{n}
comprising
n
-tuples of disjoint simple unparameterised curves which only intersect
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega
at their starting points, endowed with
the metric

\displaystyle\dist_{X}((\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{N}),(\tilde{\eta}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{\eta}_{N})):=\max_{1\leq j\leq N}\dist_{X}(\eta_{j},\tilde{\eta}_{j}).

(3.20)

Note that the metric space
(X_{0}^{n},\dist_{X})
is not complete, but
\SLE_{\kappa}
curves for
\kappa\in(0,4]
belong to this space almost surely up to any stopping time before the curves meet.

Proposition 3.4
.

Conditionally on the event
E^{\delta}_{\bm{x}}
defined in (
1.1
),
the law of
(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{1}^{\delta},\ldots,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{n}^{\delta})
converges weakly in
(X_{0}^{n},\dist_{X})
and locally uniformly
to a limit
(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{1},\ldots,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{n})

by first letting
\delta\to 0
and then shrinking
\diam(\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega)\to 0
.
Moreover, the following properties hold almost surely.

1.

Each
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{j}
is a simple curve and intersects
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega
only at its starting point, for
1\leq j\leq n
.
Moreover, the curves
(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{1}[0,t_{1}],\ldots,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{n}[0,t_{n}])
are disjoint at any times before reaching
0
.

2.

Let
z_{j}\in\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega
be the starting point of
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{j}
,
for
1\leq j\leq n
.
Then, the density of
(\phi(z_{1}),\ldots,\phi(z_{n}))

(
with respect to the product Lebesgue measure on
\partial\mathbb{D}
)

is given by (
1.8
). For every
\epsilon>0
, the conditional law of
(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{1},\ldots,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{n})
given
(z_{1},\ldots,z_{n})
stopped when hitting
\partial B(0,\epsilon)

is that of
(\eta^{\bm{z}}_{1,\epsilon},\ldots,\eta^{\bm{z}}_{n,\epsilon})
for
\kappa=2
.

Before proving Proposition
3.4
, we first give another description of the continuum curves
(\eta^{\bm{z}}_{1,\epsilon},\ldots,\eta^{\bm{z}}_{n,\epsilon})
.

Lemma 3.5
.

Fix
\kappa\in(0,4]
.
Let
\bm{z}=(z_{1},\ldots,z_{n})\in(\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega)^{n}
be
n
distinct points in counterclockwise order. Let
\bm{\varpi}^{\bm{z}}=(\varpi_{1}^{\bm{z}},\ldots,\varpi_{n}^{\bm{z}})
be
n
independent radial
\SLE_{\kappa}
curves from
\bm{z}
to
0
on
\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}}}
.
For
\epsilon>0
and
1\leq j\leq n
, let
T_{j,\epsilon}
be a stopping time for
\varpi_{j}^{\bm{z}}
before the hitting time to
B(0,\epsilon)
.
Then, the law of the curves in the inductive construction,
\bm{\eta}^{\bm{z}}[\bm{0},\bm{T}_{\epsilon}]:=(\eta^{\bm{z}}_{1,\epsilon}[0,T_{1,\epsilon}],\ldots,\eta^{\bm{z}}_{n,\epsilon}[0,T_{n,\epsilon}])
, coincides with the law of the curves

\bm{\varpi}^{\bm{z}}[\bm{0},\bm{T}_{\epsilon}]=(\varpi_{1}^{\bm{z}}[0,T_{1,\epsilon}],\ldots,\varpi_{n}^{\bm{z}}[0,T_{n,\epsilon}])

weighted by the martingale

\displaystyle R(\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}}};\bm{\varpi}^{\bm{z}}[\bm{0},\bm{T}_{\epsilon}])

\displaystyle:=\;

\displaystyle\mathbb{1}{\{I_{n}^{\epsilon}\}}\;\bigg(\frac{\psi_{\epsilon}^{\prime}(0)}{\phi^{\prime}(0)}\bigg)^{\frac{n^{2}-1}{2\kappa}+\frac{(6-\kappa)(\kappa-2)}{8\kappa}}\;\exp\left(\frac{(3\kappa-8)(6-\kappa)}{4\kappa}\,\mu_{\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}}}\setminus\bm{\varpi}^{\bm{z}}[\bm{0},\bm{T}_{\epsilon}]}\Big[(N(\ell)-1)\;\mathbb{1}{\{\ell\cap\bm{\varpi}^{\bm{z}}[\bm{0},\bm{T}_{\epsilon}]\neq\emptyset\}}\Big]\right)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{j=1}^{n}\Big(\big(\psi_{\epsilon}\circ\psi_{j,\epsilon}^{-1}\big)^{\prime}(\psi_{j,\epsilon}(\varpi_{j}^{\bm{z}}(T_{j,\epsilon})))\Big)^{\frac{6-\kappa}{2\kappa}}\;\left|\frac{\underset{1\leq i<j\leq n}{\prod}\,\sin\big(\frac{1}{2}(\arg\psi_{\epsilon}(\varpi_{j}^{\bm{z}}(T_{j,\epsilon}))-\arg\psi_{\epsilon}(\varpi_{i}^{\bm{z}}(T_{i,\epsilon})))\big)}{\underset{1\leq i<j\leq n}{\prod}\,\sin\big(\frac{1}{2}(\arg\phi(z_{j})-\arg\phi(z_{i}))\big)}\right|^{\frac{2}{\kappa}}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{j=1}^{n}\bigg(\frac{\psi^{\prime}_{j,\epsilon}(0)}{\phi^{\prime}(0)}\bigg)^{\frac{(6-\kappa)(2-\kappa)}{8\kappa}},

(3.21)

where
\psi_{\epsilon}\colon\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}}}\setminus\bm{\varpi}^{\bm{z}}[\bm{0},\bm{T}_{\epsilon}]\to\mathbb{D}

is the conformal isomorphism such that
\psi_{\epsilon}(0)=0
and
\psi^{\prime}_{\epsilon}(0)>0
,
and
\psi_{j,\epsilon}\colon\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}}}\setminus\varpi_{j}^{\bm{z}}[0,T_{j,\epsilon}]\to\mathbb{D}

is the conformal isomorphism such that
\psi_{j,\epsilon}(0)=0
and
\psi^{\prime}_{j,\epsilon}(0)>0
for each
j
,
and
N(\ell)
is the number of curves in
\{\varpi_{1}^{\bm{z}}[0,T_{1,\epsilon}],\ldots,\varpi_{n}^{\bm{z}}[0,T_{n,\epsilon}]\}
which intersect the loop
\ell
, and lastly,

\displaystyle I_{n}^{\epsilon}:=\{\varpi_{i}^{\bm{z}}[0,T_{i,\epsilon}]\cap\varpi_{j}^{\bm{z}}[0,T_{j,\epsilon}]=\emptyset\textnormal{ for }1\leq i<j\leq n\}.

In other words, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}\,\bm{\eta}^{\bm{z}}[\bm{0},\bm{T}_{\epsilon}]}{\mathrm{d}\,\bm{\varpi}^{\bm{z}}[\bm{0},\bm{T}_{\epsilon}]}=R(\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}}};\bm{\varpi}^{\bm{z}}[\bm{0},\bm{T}_{\epsilon}]),

where we use
\mathrm{d}X/\mathrm{d}\,Y
with an abuse of notation to denote the Radon–Nikodym derivative of the law of the random variable
X
with respect to that of
Y
.

Proof.

By conformal invariance, we may assume that
\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}}}=\mathbb{D}
.
Write
D_{j,\epsilon}:=\mathbb{D}\setminus\bigcup_{i=1}^{j}\eta^{\bm{z}}_{i,\epsilon}[0,T_{i,\epsilon}]
for each
j
.
Fix
j\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
.
We shall prove that, given
(z_{1},\ldots,z_{n})
, the conditional law of
\eta^{\bm{z}}_{j,\epsilon}[0,T_{j,\epsilon}]
given
\eta^{\bm{z}}_{1,\epsilon}[0,T_{1,\epsilon}],\ldots,\eta^{\bm{z}}_{j-1,\epsilon}[0,T_{j-1,\epsilon}]
is that of
\varpi_{j}^{\bm{z}}[0,T_{j,\epsilon}]
weighted by

\displaystyle\frac{R(\mathbb{D};\eta^{\bm{z}}_{1,\epsilon}[0,T_{1,\epsilon}],\ldots,\eta^{\bm{z}}_{j-1,\epsilon}[0,T_{j-1,\epsilon}],\varpi_{j}^{\bm{z}}[0,T_{j,\epsilon}],z_{j+1},\ldots,z_{n})}{R(\mathbb{D};\eta^{\bm{z}}_{1,\epsilon}[0,T_{1,\epsilon}],\ldots,\eta^{\bm{z}}_{j-1,\epsilon}[0,T_{j-1,\epsilon}],z_{j},\ldots,z_{n})}.

(3.22)

Denote by
\phi_{j,\epsilon}\colon D_{j,\epsilon}\to\mathbb{D}
the conformal isomorphism such that
\phi_{j,\epsilon}(0)=0
and
\phi_{j,\epsilon}^{\prime}(0)>0
.
Denote by
\eta_{j}
the radial
\SLE_{\kappa}
from
z_{j}
to
0
on
D_{j-1,\epsilon}
.
Then, by
[
HL21
, Proposition 2.2]
, it follows that the Radon–Nikodym derivative of
\eta_{j}
with respect to
\varpi_{j}^{\bm{z}}
is given by

\displaystyle J(\eta^{\bm{z}}_{1,\epsilon}[0,T_{1,\epsilon}],\ldots,\eta^{\bm{z}}_{j-1,\epsilon}[0,T_{j-1,\epsilon}];\varpi_{j}^{\bm{z}}[0,T_{j,\epsilon}])

(3.23)

\displaystyle:=\;

\displaystyle\mathbb{1}{\Big\{\varpi_{j}^{\bm{z}}[0,T_{j,\epsilon}]\cap\bigcup_{i=1}^{j-1}\eta^{\bm{z}}_{i,\epsilon}[0,T_{i,\epsilon}]=\emptyset\Big\}}\bigg(\frac{(\phi_{j,\epsilon}\circ\psi_{j,\epsilon}^{-1})^{\prime}(\psi_{j,\epsilon}(\varpi_{j}^{\bm{z}}(T_{j,\epsilon})))}{\phi^{\prime}_{j-1,\epsilon}(z_{j})}\bigg)^{\frac{6-\kappa}{2\kappa}}\bigg(\frac{(\phi_{j,\epsilon}\circ\psi_{j,\epsilon}^{-1})^{\prime}(0)}{\phi^{\prime}_{j-1,\epsilon}(0)}\bigg)^{\frac{(6-\kappa)(\kappa-2)}{8\kappa}}

\displaystyle\times\exp\bigg(\frac{(3\kappa-8)(6-\kappa)}{4\kappa}\;\mu_{D_{j-1,\epsilon}}\Big[\ell\in\mathcal{L}\textnormal{ such that }\ell\cap\bigcup_{i=1}^{j-1}\eta^{\bm{z}}_{i,\epsilon}[0,T_{i,\epsilon}]\neq\emptyset\textnormal{ and }\ell\cap\varpi_{j}^{\bm{z}}[0,T_{j,\epsilon}]\neq\emptyset\Big]\bigg)

Next, denote

\displaystyle(w_{1},\ldots,w_{n-1}):=(\phi_{j-1,\epsilon}(\eta^{\bm{z}}_{1,\epsilon}(T_{1,\epsilon})),\ldots,\phi_{j-1,\epsilon}(\eta^{\bm{z}}_{j-1,\epsilon}(T_{j-1,\epsilon})),\phi_{j-1,\epsilon}(z_{j}),\ldots,\phi_{j-1,\epsilon}(z_{n})),

and denote by
(\tilde{\Theta}\colon t\geq 0)
the driving function of
\phi_{j-1,\epsilon}(\eta_{j})
and by
(\tilde{g}_{t}\colon t\geq 0)
the corresponding radial Loewner flow as in (
3.18
).
By Itô’s formula, the process
\{M(t\wedge T_{j,\epsilon})\}_{t\geq 0}
defined by

\displaystyle M(t):=\;

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{\frac{n^{2}-1}{2\kappa}t}\;\prod_{i=1}^{n-1}|\tilde{g}^{\prime}_{t}(w_{i})|^{\frac{6-\kappa}{2\kappa}}\;\prod_{i=1}^{n-1}\bigg|\sin\left(\frac{\arg\tilde{g}_{t}(w_{i})-\tilde{\Theta}(t)}{2}\right)\bigg|^{\frac{2}{\kappa}}\prod_{1\leq i<l\leq n-1}\bigg|\sin\left(\frac{\arg\tilde{g}_{t}(w_{l})-\arg\tilde{g}_{t}(w_{i})}{2}\right)\bigg|^{\frac{2}{\kappa}}

is a martingale for
\phi_{j-1,\epsilon}(\eta_{j})
.
By Girsanov’s theorem,
the conditional law of
\phi_{j-1,\epsilon}(\eta^{\bm{z}}_{j,\epsilon}[0,T_{j,\epsilon}])
given
\eta^{\bm{z}}_{1,\epsilon}[0,T_{1,\epsilon}],\ldots,\eta^{\bm{z}}_{j-1,\epsilon}[0,T_{j-1,\epsilon}]
is that of

\phi_{j-1,\epsilon}(\varpi_{j}^{\bm{z}}[0,T_{j,\epsilon}])
tilted by
M(T_{j,\epsilon})
.
Combining with (
3.23
), we obtain (
3.22
).
Lastly, taking the product of (
3.22
) over
1\leq j\leq n
, we complete the proof.
∎

We now consider the case of one curve,
n=1
, where the limit
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{1}
, if stopped when hitting
\partial B(0,\epsilon)
at time
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}
,
is just a radial
\SLE_{2}
curve
\eta
stopped at the same
10
10

10

For simplicity of notation, we will denote the stopping times for various curves by the same symbol, though for different curves the hitting time has different values. We will parameterise curves by capacity.

hitting time
\tau_{\epsilon}
,
so that
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{1}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}]\sim\eta[0,\tau_{\epsilon}]
.

Proof of Proposition
3.4
,
n=1
.

Obtaining tightness of
\{\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{1}^{\delta}\}_{\delta>0}
in
(X,\dist_{X})
is standard
[
LSW04
, Theorem 3.9]
.
The input of the proof is the Beurling estimate for simple random walk (see
[
BL23
, Lemma 3.2]

for the proof with the presence of a reflective boundary).
Suppose
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{1}\in X
is any subsequential limit.
To show the convergence of the whole sequence, we will prove that for every
\epsilon>\diam(\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega)
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\diam(\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega)\to 0}\big|\big|\,\eta[0,\tau_{\epsilon}]-\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{1}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}]\,\big|\big|_{\rm TV}=0.

(3.24)

Let
\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}}}^{\delta}
be the discrete simply connected domain bounded by
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega^{\delta}
on
\delta\mathbb{Z}^{2}
.
For
w^{\delta}\in\Omega^{\delta}
, denote by
\Gamma_{w}^{\delta}
(resp.
\Gamma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}},w}^{\delta}
) the loop-erasure of a random walk
\mathcal{R}\sim\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{w}
on
\Omega^{\delta}
(resp.
\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}}}^{\delta}
) starting from
w^{\delta}
and ending at
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega^{\delta}
, reflected at
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega^{\delta}
.
Denote by
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{w}^{\delta}
(resp.
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}},w}^{\delta}
) the time-reversal of
\Gamma_{w}^{\delta}
(resp.
\Gamma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}},w}^{\delta}
).

Let
s>0
be such that
\diam(\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega^{\delta})<\frac{s}{2}
for all
\delta
small enough.
Take
w\in\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}}}
such that
\dist(w,0)=s
and
w^{\delta}
on
\Omega^{\delta}
such that
|w-w^{\delta}|\leq 2\delta
.
Since the random walk converges to Brownian motion, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\limsup_{\delta\to 0}\big|\big|\,\Gamma_{w}^{\delta}-\Gamma_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}},w}^{\delta}\,\big|\big|_{\rm TV}\leq\;&\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{w}\big[\mathcal{R}\textnormal{ hits }\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega^{\delta}\textnormal{ before hitting }\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega^{\delta}\big]\\
\leq\;&\frac{\log(s/\dist(0,\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega))}{\log(\diam(\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega)/\dist(0,\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega))}.\end{split}

(3.25)

By Wilson’s algorithm (Theorem
2.1
),
for every discrete simple curve
\varrho^{\delta}
on
\Omega^{\delta}
started at
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega^{\delta}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\delta}\big[\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{1}^{\delta}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}]=\varrho^{\delta}\big]=\mathbb{E}^{\delta}\Bigg[\frac{\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{x_{1}}[\mathcal{R}\textnormal{ hits }\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega^{\delta}\cup\varrho^{\delta}\textnormal{ at }\varrho^{\delta}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta})]}{\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{w}[\mathcal{R}\textnormal{ hits }\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega^{\delta}\cup\varrho^{\delta}\textnormal{ at }\varrho^{\delta}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta})]}\;\mathbb{1}{\big\{\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{w}^{\delta}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}]=\varrho^{\delta}\big\}}\Bigg],

where
x_{1}^{\delta}\in\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega^{\delta}\subset B(0,\epsilon)
is the eventual endpoint of
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{1}^{\delta}
.
Thus, the Radon–Nikodym derivative of
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{1}^{\delta}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}]
with respect to
