\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{w}^{\delta}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}]
is given by

\displaystyle A^{\delta}\big(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{w}^{\delta}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}]\big):=\frac{\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{x_{1}}[\mathcal{R}\textnormal{ hits }\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega^{\delta}\cup\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{w}^{\delta}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}]\textnormal{ at }\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{w}^{\delta}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta})]}{\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{w}[\mathcal{R}\textnormal{ hits }\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega^{\delta}\cup\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{w}^{\delta}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}]\textnormal{ at }\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{w}^{\delta}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta})]}.

By a Harnack-type inequality, there exist constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
such that

\displaystyle\big|A^{\delta}\big(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{w}^{\delta}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}]\big)-1\big|\leq C_{1}\Big(\frac{s}{\epsilon}\Big)^{C_{2}},

for every realisation of
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{w}^{\delta}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}]
.
This implies that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\delta\to 0}\big|\big|\,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{1}^{\delta}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}]-\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{w}^{\delta}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}]\,\big|\big|_{\rm TV}\leq C_{1}\Big(\frac{s}{\epsilon}\Big)^{C_{2}}.

(3.26)

Next, choose a discrete approximation
o^{\delta}
of
0
on
\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}}}^{\delta}
.
By the discrete Beurling estimate, there exist constants
C_{3}>0
and
C_{4}>0
such that in
\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}}}^{\delta}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\delta}\Big[\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}},o}^{\delta}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}},\epsilon}^{\delta}]\neq\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}},w}^{\delta}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}},\epsilon}^{\delta}]\Big]\leq C_{3}\Big(\frac{s}{\epsilon}\Big)^{C_{4}}.

By the well-known result in
[
LSW04
, Theorem 3.9]
,
the LERW
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}},o}^{\delta}
on
\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}}}^{\delta}
converges in law to the radial
\SLE_{2}
curve
\eta
both in
(X,\dist_{X})
and locally uniformly.
In particular, combining with (
3.25
,
3.26
), we conclude that
there exist constants
C_{5}>0
and
C_{6}>0
such that we have

\displaystyle\big|\big|\,\eta[0,\tau_{\epsilon}]-\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{1}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}]\,\big|\big|_{\rm TV}\leq C_{5}\Big(\frac{s}{\epsilon}\Big)^{C_{6}}.

(3.27)

Taking
s\to 0
along with
\diam(\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega)\to 0
, this shows (
3.24
) and completes the proof of the identification of the scaling limit.
The properties
1
&
2
follow from properties of the radial
\SLE_{2}

[
LSW04
,
RS05
]
.
∎

The next lemma establishes tightness of the multiple UST branches.

Lemma 3.6
.

Conditionally on the event
E^{\delta}_{\bm{x}}
,
the family
\{(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{1}^{\delta},\ldots,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{n}^{\delta})\}_{\delta>0}
is tight in
(X_{0}^{n},\dist_{X})
.

Proof.

Take disjoint subdomains
U_{1},\ldots,U_{n}
of
\Omega
such that
x_{j}\in\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega\cap U_{j}
and
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega\cap\partial U_{j}\neq\emptyset
,
and discrete approximations
U_{1}^{\delta},\ldots,U_{n}^{\delta}
on
\Omega^{\delta}
such that

x_{j}^{\delta}\in\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega^{\delta}\cap U_{j}^{\delta}
and
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega^{\delta}\cap\partial U_{j}^{\delta}\neq\emptyset
,
and
\dist(\partial U_{j}^{\delta},\partial U_{j})\to 0
as
\delta\to 0
, for
1\leq j\leq n
.
There exists a constant
C=C(\Omega)>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\delta}[E^{\delta}_{\bm{x}}]\;\geq\;\prod_{j=1}^{n}\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{x_{j}}[\mathcal{R}\textnormal{ hits }\partial U_{j}^{\delta}\cap\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega^{\delta}\textnormal{ when it exits }U_{j}^{\delta}]\;\geq\;C

(3.28)

for all
\delta
small enough.
Thus, the tightness of
\{(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{1}^{\delta},\ldots,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{n}^{\delta})\}_{\delta>0}

in
(X_{0}^{n},\dist_{X})
is readily obtained from the case of
n=1
,
and any subsequential limit comprises simple curves
(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{1},\ldots,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{n})
.
To show that they are also disjoint, by symmetry it suffices to show that
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{1}
avoids the other curves.
Indeed, the discrete Beurling estimate implies that there exist constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\delta}\big[\mathbb{P}^{\delta}\big[\{\gamma_{j}^{\delta}\textnormal{ hits the }\epsilon\textnormal{-neighbourhood of }\gamma_{1}^{\delta}\}\cap E^{\delta}_{\bm{x}}\,|\,\gamma_{1}^{\delta}]\big]

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{x_{j}}[\mathcal{R}\textnormal{ ends at }\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega^{\delta}\textnormal{ and hits the }\epsilon\textnormal{-neighbourhood of }\gamma_{1}^{\delta}]\;\leq\;C_{1}\prod_{i=2}^{n}\epsilon^{C_{2}},\qquad\epsilon>0.

By letting
\delta\to 0
, we find that
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{1}\cap\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{j}=\emptyset
almost surely for all
2\leq j\leq n
.
Thus,
(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{1},\ldots,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{n})\in X_{0}^{n}
.
∎

We are now ready to finish the proof of Proposition
3.4
.
We use the same notation as in the above proofs, and recall Wilson’s algorithm (Theorem
2.1
) relating LERW to the UST branches.

Proof of Proposition
3.4
,
n\geq 2
.

By conformal invariance, we may assume that
\Omega=\mathbb{A}_{r}
.
We proceed by induction on
n\geq 1
. The base case
n=1
was established above.
Fix
k\geq 2
and suppose that the claims in Proposition
3.4
hold for all
1\leq n\leq k-1
.
For
k
disjoint simple discrete curves
(\varrho^{\delta}_{1},\ldots,\varrho^{\delta}_{k})
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\delta}\big[(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{1}^{\delta}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}],\ldots,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{k}^{\delta}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}])=(\varrho_{1}^{\delta},\ldots,\varrho_{k}^{\delta})\big]

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathbb{P}^{\delta}[E^{\delta}_{\bm{x}}]}\frac{\#\{\textnormal{spanning trees $\mathcal{T}^{\delta}$ on }\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}\textnormal{ such that }\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\gamma}$}}$}{}^{\delta}\in E^{\delta}_{\bm{x}}\textnormal{ and }(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{1}^{\delta}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}],\ldots,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{k}^{\delta}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}])=(\varrho_{1}^{\delta},\ldots,\varrho_{k}^{\delta})\}}{\#\{\textnormal{spanning trees $\mathcal{T}^{\delta}$ on }\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}\}}

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle h_{\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta},\bm{\varrho}^{\delta}}(x_{1}^{\delta},\varrho_{1}^{\delta}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}))\;\frac{M(\bm{\varrho}^{\delta};\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta})}{p^{\delta}(\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta};\bm{x}^{\delta};\hat{\bm{x}}^{\delta})}\;\frac{\#\{\textnormal{spanning trees $\mathcal{T}^{\delta}$ on }\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}\cup\bm{\varrho}^{\delta}\}}{\#\{\textnormal{spanning trees $\mathcal{T}^{\delta}$ on }\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}\cup\hat{\bm{\varrho}}^{\delta}\}}

\displaystyle\times\frac{\mathbb{P}^{\delta}[E(\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}\cup\hat{\bm{\varrho}}^{\delta};x_{2}^{\delta},\ldots,x_{k}^{\delta};\varrho_{2}^{\delta}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}),\ldots,\varrho_{k}^{\delta}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}))]}{\mathbb{P}^{\delta}[E^{\delta}_{\hat{\bm{x}}}]}\;\frac{\#\{\textnormal{spanning trees $\mathcal{T}^{\delta}$ on }\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}\cup\hat{\bm{\varrho}}^{\delta}\}}{\#\{\textnormal{spanning trees $\mathcal{T}^{\delta}$ on }\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}\}},

where
\epsilon>2r
,
and we denote by
\bm{\varrho}^{\delta}=\bigcup_{j=1}^{k}\varrho_{j}^{\delta}
,
and
\hat{\bm{\varrho}}^{\delta}=\bigcup_{j=2}^{k}\varrho_{j}^{\delta}
, and
by
\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}\cup\bm{\varrho}^{\delta}
(resp.
\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}\cup\hat{\bm{\varrho}}^{\delta}
)
the graph obtained by wiring the outer boundary
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}
and
\bm{\varrho}^{\delta}
(resp.
\hat{\bm{\varrho}}^{\delta}
) together;
and where

\displaystyle h_{\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}\cup\bm{\varrho}^{\delta}}(x_{1}^{\delta},\varrho_{1}^{\delta}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta})):=\;

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{x_{1}}\big[\mathcal{R}\textnormal{ hits }\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}\cup\bm{\varrho}^{\delta}\textnormal{ through }\varrho_{1}^{\delta}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta})\big]\;\in\;(0,1),

\displaystyle M(\bm{\varrho}^{\delta};\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}):=\;

\displaystyle\frac{1}{h_{\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}\cup\bm{\varrho}^{\delta}}(x_{1}^{\delta},\varrho_{1}^{\delta}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}))}\;\frac{\mathbb{P}^{\delta}[E(\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}\cup\bm{\varrho}^{\delta};x_{1}^{\delta},\ldots,x_{k}^{\delta};\varrho_{1}^{\delta}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}),\ldots,\varrho_{k}^{\delta}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}))]}{\mathbb{P}^{\delta}[E(\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}\cup\hat{\bm{\varrho}}^{\delta};x_{2}^{\delta},\ldots,x_{k}^{\delta};\varrho_{2}^{\delta}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}),\ldots,\varrho_{k}^{\delta}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}))]}\;\in\;(0,1),

\displaystyle p^{\delta}(\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta};\bm{x}^{\delta};\hat{\bm{x}}^{\delta}):=\;

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{P}^{\delta}[E^{\delta}_{\bm{x}}]}{\mathbb{P}^{\delta}[E^{\delta}_{\hat{\bm{x}}}]}\;=\;\frac{\mathbb{P}^{\delta}[E(\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta};x_{1}^{\delta},\ldots,x_{k}^{\delta})]}{\mathbb{P}^{\delta}[E(\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta};x_{2}^{\delta},\ldots,x_{k}^{\delta})]}\;\in\;(0,1).

Consider now random curves
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\Gamma}$}}$}{}^{\delta}=(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{1}^{\delta},\ldots,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{k}^{\delta})
such that

•

the law of
(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{2}^{\delta},\ldots,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{k}^{\delta})
is the conditional law of
(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{2}^{\delta},\ldots,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{k}^{\delta})
given the event
E^{\delta}_{\hat{\bm{x}}}=E(\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta};x_{2}^{\delta},\ldots,x_{k}^{\delta})
;

•

given
(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{2}^{\delta},\ldots,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{k}^{\delta})
, the law of
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{1}^{\delta}
is that of the time-reversed LERW
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{x_{1}}^{\delta}
starting from
x_{1}^{\delta}
and ending at
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}\cup\bigcup_{j=2}^{k}\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{j}^{\delta}
, reflected at
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}
.

Then, the Radon–Nikodym derivative of

\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\gamma}$}}$}{}^{\delta}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}]=(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{1}^{\delta}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}],\ldots,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{k}^{\delta}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}])
with respect to
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\Gamma}$}}$}{}^{\delta}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}]
is

\displaystyle L(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\Gamma}$}}$}{}^{\delta}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}]):=\frac{M(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\Gamma}$}}$}{}^{\delta}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}];\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta})}{p^{\delta}(\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta};\bm{x}^{\delta};\hat{\bm{x}}^{\delta})}\;\mathbb{1}{\Big\{\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}^{\delta}_{1}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}]\cap\bigcup_{j=2}^{k}\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}^{\delta}_{j}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}]=\emptyset\Big\}}\;\mathbb{1}{\big\{(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}^{\delta}_{1}(0),\ldots,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}^{\delta}_{k}(0))\in\mathcal{X}_{k}\big\}}.

Similarly to (
3.28
), there exists a constant
C=C(r)>0
, which only depends on
r
, such that

\displaystyle p^{\delta}(\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta};\bm{x}^{\delta};\hat{\bm{x}}^{\delta})\geq\mathbb{P}^{\delta}[E^{\delta}_{\bm{x}}]\geq C.

Combining this with the fact that
M(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\Gamma}$}}$}{}_{1}^{\delta}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}];\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta})\leq 1
, we see that
L(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\Gamma}$}}$}{}^{\delta}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}])\leq C
.

Lemma
3.6
implies that both families

\{\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\Gamma}$}}$}{}^{\delta}\}_{\delta>0}
and
\{\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\gamma}$}}$}{}^{\delta}\}_{\delta>0}
are tight in
(X_{0}^{k},\dist_{X})
.
It follows similarly as in the proof of
[
LSW04
, Theorem 3.9]

that the families are also tight in the uniform topology of curves with common parametrisation.
Take any respective subsequential limits
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\Gamma}$}}$}{}^{(r)}=(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{1}^{(r)},\ldots,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{k}^{(r)})
and
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\gamma}$}}$}{}^{(r)}=(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{1}^{(r)},\ldots,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{k}^{(r)})
.
By Skorokhod’s representation theorem, without loss of generality we may assume that along subsequences,

\{\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\Gamma}$}}$}{}^{\delta}\}_{\delta>0}
and
\{\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\gamma}$}}$}{}^{\delta}\}_{\delta>0}

converge almost surely to
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\Gamma}$}}$}{}^{(r)}
and
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\gamma}$}}$}{}^{(r)}
, respectively, in both
(X_{0}^{k},\dist_{X})
and locally uniformly.
The induction hypothesis now implies the following facts.

•

We have

\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\Gamma}$}}$}{}^{(r)}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}]\to\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\Gamma}$}}$}{}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}]
as
r\to 0
in total variation distance.
In particular, there exists a coupling
\mathcal{Q}
such that almost surely, for every
t\geq 0
, the curves
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\Gamma}$}}$}{}^{(r)}[0,t]
agree with the curves
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\Gamma}$}}$}{}[0,t]
when
r>0
is small enough.

•

The conditional law of
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{1}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}]
given
(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{2}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}],\ldots,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{k}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}])
is that of
the radial
\SLE_{2}
curve
\varpi_{1}[0,\tau_{\epsilon}]
in
\hat{D}_{\epsilon}:=\mathbb{D}\setminus\bigcup_{j=2}^{k}\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{j}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}]
from
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{1}(0)
to the origin.

•

The conditional law of the starting point
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{1}(0)
is given by the harmonic measure in
\hat{D}_{\epsilon}
seen from
0
.
Thus, its density on
\mathbb{S}=\partial\mathbb{D}
equals

\displaystyle\rho(z_{1})=\frac{\hat{\phi}^{\prime}_{\epsilon}(z_{1})}{2\pi}\;\mathbb{1}{\big\{(z_{1},\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{2}(0),\ldots,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{k}(0))\in\mathcal{X}_{k}\big\}},

where
\hat{\phi}_{\epsilon}\colon\hat{D}_{\epsilon}\to\mathbb{D}
is the uniformising map such that
\hat{\phi}_{\epsilon}(0)=0
and
\hat{\phi}_{\epsilon}^{\prime}(0)>0
.

We will also consider the uniformising map
\phi_{\epsilon}\colon\mathbb{D}\setminus\bigcup_{j=1}^{k}\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{j}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}]\to\mathbb{D}
such that
\phi_{\epsilon}(0)=0
and
\phi_{\epsilon}^{\prime}(0)>0
,
and the conformal isomorphisms

•

\phi^{(r)}_{\epsilon}\colon\mathbb{A}_{r}\setminus\bigcup_{j=1}^{k}\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{j}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}]\to\mathbb{A}_{r_{\epsilon}}
, such that
\phi^{(r)}_{\epsilon}(r)=:r({\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}})=:r_{\epsilon}
, so that
\frac{r_{\epsilon}}{r}\to\phi_{\epsilon}^{\prime}(0)
as
r\to 0
; and

•

\hat{\phi}^{(r)}_{\epsilon}\colon\mathbb{A}_{r}\setminus\bigcup_{j=2}^{k}\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{j}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}]\to\mathbb{A}_{\hat{r}_{\epsilon}}
, such that
\hat{\phi}^{(r)}_{\epsilon}(r)=:\hat{r}({\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}})=:\hat{r}_{\epsilon}
, so that
\frac{\hat{r}_{\epsilon}}{r}\to\hat{\phi}_{\epsilon}^{\prime}(0)
as
r\to 0
.

Next, as in Proposition
3.1
and Lemma
2.12
,
one can prove that

M(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\Gamma}$}}$}{}_{1}^{\delta}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}];\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta})
converges almost surely to a limit
M_{r}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\Gamma}$}}$}{}_{1}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}];\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon})\leq 1
as
\delta\to 0
,
and
p^{\delta}(\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta};\bm{x}^{\delta};\hat{\bm{x}}^{\delta})\to p(\mathbb{A}_{r};\bm{x};\hat{\bm{x}})

— so by the dominated convergence theorem, the Radon–Nikodym derivative of

\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\gamma}$}}$}{}^{(r)}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}]
with respect to
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\Gamma}$}}$}{}^{(r)}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}]
is

\displaystyle L_{r}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\Gamma}$}}$}{}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}]):=\frac{M_{r}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\Gamma}$}}$}{}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}];\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon})}{p(\mathbb{A}_{r};\bm{x};\hat{\bm{x}})}\;\mathbb{1}{\Big\{\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{1}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}]\cap\bigcup_{j=2}^{k}\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{j}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}]=\emptyset\Big\}}\;\mathbb{1}{\big\{(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{1}(0),\ldots,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{k}(0))\in\mathcal{X}_{k}\big\}}.

A computation similar to the one presented in the proof of Lemma
A.1
yields

\displaystyle\frac{\underset{1\leq i<j\leq k}{\prod}\sin\Big(\frac{1}{2}\big(\arg\phi_{\epsilon}^{(r)}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{j}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}))-\arg\phi_{\epsilon}^{(r)}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{i}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}))\big)\Big)}{\underset{2\leq i<j\leq k}{\prod}\sin\Big(\frac{1}{2}\big(\arg\hat{\phi}_{\epsilon}^{(r)}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{j}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}))-\arg\hat{\phi}_{\epsilon}^{(r)}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{i}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}))\big)\Big)}\;

\displaystyle\;\overset{r\to 0}{\longrightarrow}\;\frac{\underset{1\leq i<j\leq k}{\prod}\sin\Big(\frac{1}{2}\big(\arg\phi_{\epsilon}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{j}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}))-\arg\phi_{\epsilon}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{i}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}))\big)\Big)}{\underset{2\leq i<j\leq k}{\prod}\sin\Big(\frac{1}{2}\big(\arg\hat{\phi}_{\epsilon}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{j}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}))-\arg\hat{\phi}_{\epsilon}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{i}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}))\big)\Big)}

\displaystyle\underset{1\leq i<j\leq k}{\prod}\sin\Big(\tfrac{1}{2}\big(\arg\phi_{\epsilon}^{(r)}(x_{j})-\arg\phi_{\epsilon}^{(r)}(x_{i})\big)\Big)\;

\displaystyle\;\overset{r\to 0}{\longrightarrow}\;\underset{1\leq i<j\leq k}{\prod}\sin\Big(\tfrac{1}{2}\big(\arg x_{j}-\arg x_{i}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\underset{2\leq i<j\leq k}{\prod}\sin\Big(\tfrac{1}{2}\big(\arg\hat{\phi}_{\epsilon}^{(r)}(x_{j})-\arg\hat{\phi}_{\epsilon}^{(r)}(x_{i})\big)\Big)\;

\displaystyle\;\overset{r\to 0}{\longrightarrow}\;\underset{2\leq i<j\leq k}{\prod}\sin\Big(\tfrac{1}{2}\big(\arg x_{j}-\arg x_{i}\big)\Big)

Combining this with Proposition
3.1
, Lemma
2.11
&
2.12
,
and Equation (
3.9
) in Proposition
3.2

(also proven in Lemma
A.1
in Appendix
A
), we find that

\displaystyle L_{r}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\Gamma}$}}$}{}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}])=\;

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\,\frac{\mathcal{Z}_{k-1}}{\mathcal{Z}_{k}}\;(1+o_{r}(1))\;\;\mathbb{1}{\Big\{\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{1}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}]\cap\bigcup_{j=2}^{k}\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{j}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}]=\emptyset\Big\}}\;\mathbb{1}{\big\{(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{1}(0),\ldots,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{k}(0))\in\mathcal{X}_{k}\big\}}

\displaystyle\times\;

\displaystyle\frac{(\phi_{\epsilon}^{\prime}(0))^{\frac{k^{2}-1}{4}}}{(\hat{\phi}_{\epsilon}^{\prime}(0))^{\frac{(k-1)^{2}-1}{4}}}\;\prod_{j=2}^{k}\big|(\phi_{\epsilon}\circ\hat{\phi}_{\epsilon}^{-1})^{\prime}(\hat{\phi}_{\epsilon}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{j}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon})))\big|\frac{\underset{1\leq i<j\leq k}{\prod}\sin\Big(\frac{1}{2}\big(\arg\phi_{\epsilon}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{j}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}))-\arg\phi_{\epsilon}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{i}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}))\big)\Big)}{\underset{2\leq i<j\leq k}{\prod}\sin\Big(\frac{1}{2}\big(\arg\hat{\phi}_{\epsilon}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{j}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}))-\arg\hat{\phi}_{\epsilon}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{i}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}))\big)\Big)}.

By Fatou’s lemma, under the coupling
\mathcal{Q}
, we have

\displaystyle 1=\liminf_{r\to 0}\mathcal{Q}\big[L_{r}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\Gamma}$}}$}{}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}])\big]\geq\mathcal{Q}\big[L(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\Gamma}$}}$}{}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}])\big],

(3.29)

where
L_{r}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\Gamma}$}}$}{}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}])\to L(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\Gamma}$}}$}{}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}])\leq C
as
r\to 0
, almost surely.

We use Lemma
3.5
to identify the limit.
Let
\bm{\varpi}^{\bm{z}}:=(\varpi_{1}^{z_{1}},\ldots,\varpi_{k}^{z_{k}})
be
k
independent radial
\SLE_{\kappa}
curves on
\mathbb{D}
in the common parametrisation started at
\bm{z}=(z_{1},\ldots,z_{k})
. For any bounded continuous function
f
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}\big[f(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\Gamma}$}}$}{}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}])\,L(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\Gamma}$}}$}{}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}])\big]

(3.30)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\frac{\mathcal{Z}_{k-1}}{\mathcal{Z}_{k}}\int_{\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\rho(z_{1})\,f(\varpi_{1}^{z_{1}}[0,\tau_{\epsilon}],\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{2}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}],\ldots,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{k}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}])\,L(\varpi_{1}^{z_{1}}[0,\tau_{\epsilon}],\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{2}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}],\ldots,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{k}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}])

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\times J(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{2}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}],\ldots,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{k}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}];\varpi_{1}^{z_{1}}[0,\tau_{\epsilon}])\Big]\;|\mathrm{d}z_{1}|\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\;\;\textnormal{[by~\eqref{eqn::RN_IM_2}~\&~symm.]}

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathcal{Z}_{k}}\int_{\mathcal{X}_{k}}\underset{2\leq i<j\leq k}{\prod}\sin\Big(\frac{\arg z_{j}-\arg z_{i}}{2}\Big)\;\mathbb{E}\Big[f(\bm{\varpi}^{\bm{z}}[0,\tau_{\epsilon}])\,S(\bm{\varpi}^{\bm{z}}[0,\tau_{\epsilon}])\Big]\;\prod_{j=1}^{k}|\mathrm{d}z_{j}|,\qquad\;\textnormal{[ind.~hypo.~\&~Lem~\ref{lem::flowline_nsided}]}

where using the notation from (
3.23
,
3.5
), we have

\displaystyle S(\bm{\varpi}^{\bm{z}}[0,\tau_{\epsilon}]):=\rho(z_{1})\,L(\bm{\varpi}^{\bm{z}}[0,\tau_{\epsilon}])\,J(\bm{\varpi}^{\bm{z}}[0,\tau_{\epsilon}])\,R(\mathbb{D};\varpi_{2}^{z_{2}}[0,\tau_{\epsilon}],\ldots,\varpi_{k}^{z_{k}}[0,\tau_{\epsilon}]).

A direct computation shows that

\displaystyle S(\bm{\varpi}^{\bm{z}}[0,\tau_{\epsilon}])=\frac{\underset{1\leq i<j\leq k}{\prod}\sin\Big(\tfrac{1}{2}\big(\arg z_{j}-\arg z_{i}\big)\Big)}{\underset{2\leq i<j\leq k}{\prod}\sin\Big(\tfrac{1}{2}\big(\arg z_{j}-\arg z_{i}\big)\Big)}\;R(\mathbb{D};\bm{\varpi}^{\bm{z}}[0,\tau_{\epsilon}]),

where
\bm{\varpi}^{\bm{z}}[0,\tau_{\epsilon}]=(\varpi_{1}^{z_{1}}[0,\tau_{\epsilon}],\varpi_{2}^{z_{2}}[0,\tau_{\epsilon}],\ldots,\varpi_{k}^{z_{k}}[0,\tau_{\epsilon}])
.
Taking
f\equiv 1
, Lemma
3.5
yields

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}\big[L(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\Gamma}$}}$}{}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}])\big]=1,

(3.31)

and we see that weighted by
L(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\Gamma}$}}$}{}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}])
,
the law of
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\Gamma}$}}$}{}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}]
satisfies the properties
1
&
2
in the statement of Proposition
3.4
.
Moreover, by (
3.29
,
3.31
), under the coupling
\mathcal{Q}
, the family
\{L_{r}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\Gamma}$}}$}{}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}])\}_{r>0}
is uniformly integrable.
Hence, we deduce that the law of
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\gamma}$}}$}{}^{(r)}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}]
also converges as
r\to 0
in total variation distance:

\displaystyle\lim_{r\to 0}\big|\big|\,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\Gamma}$}}$}{}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}]-\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\gamma}$}}$}{}^{(r)}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}]\,\big|\big|_{\rm TV}=\;

\displaystyle\lim_{r\to 0}\sup_{A}\Big|\mathbb{P}\big[\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\Gamma}$}}$}{}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}]\in A\big]-\mathbb{P}\big[\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\gamma}$}}$}{}^{(r)}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}]\in A\big]\Big|

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\lim_{r\to 0}\sup_{A}\Big|\mathcal{Q}\big[\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\Gamma}$}}$}{}^{(r)}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}]\in A\big]-\mathcal{Q}\big[L_{r}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\Gamma}$}}$}{}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}])\,\mathbb{1}{\{\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\Gamma}$}}$}{}^{(r)}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}]\in A\}}\big]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\lim_{r\to 0}\Big|\mathcal{Q}\big[|1-L_{r}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\bm{\Gamma}$}}$}{}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}])|\big]\Big|\;=\;0

by the dominated convergence theorem and the choice of the coupling.
This finishes the induction step.
∎

We now turn to the reparameterised discrete curves
(\eta_{1}^{\delta},\ldots,\eta_{n}^{\delta})
in
\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}}}=\mathbb{D}
.
To prove Theorem
1.2
, it suffices to prove that their limit has the same law as the
n
-sided radial
\SLE_{2}
curves
\bm{\eta}[0,\tau_{\epsilon}]
. We will, in fact, prove a more general result.

Lemma 3.7
.

Fix
\kappa\in(0,4]
.
Let
\bm{z}\in\mathbb{S}^{n}
be
n
distinct points in counterclockwise order.
Fix
\epsilon>0
.
Let the curves
(\eta^{\bm{z}}_{1,\epsilon},\ldots,\eta^{\bm{z}}_{n,\epsilon})
be reparameterised by common parametrisation, to obtain
(\hat{\eta}^{\bm{z}}_{1,\epsilon},\ldots,\hat{\eta}^{\bm{z}}_{n,\epsilon})
.
Then, up to the hitting time
\tau_{\epsilon}
to
\partial B(0,\epsilon)
, the law of
(\hat{\eta}^{\bm{z}}_{1,\epsilon},\ldots,\hat{\eta}^{\bm{z}}_{n,\epsilon})
is that of the
n
-sided radial
\SLE_{\kappa}
.

Proof.

Let
\bm{\varpi}=(\varpi_{1},\ldots,\varpi_{n})
be
n
independent radial
\SLE_{\kappa}
curves from
\bm{z}
to
0
on
\mathbb{D}
,
and
\bm{\eta}=(\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{n})
an
n
-sided radial
\SLE_{\kappa}
from
\bm{z}
to
0
on
\mathbb{D}
,
both with the common parametrisation.
For each
t\geq 0
, by
[
HL21
, Propositions 3.4 & 3.6]

the Radon–Nikodym derivative of
\bm{\eta}[0,t]
with respect to
\bm{\varpi}[0,t]
is given by
the same formal expression
R(\mathbb{D};\bm{\varpi}[0,t])
in Equation (
3.5
) in Lemma
3.5
.
The difficulty is that we cannot stop the
n
-sided SLE (run with common parametrisation) at
a stopping time in such a way that all
n
curves stop when they first reach
\partial B(0,\epsilon)
,
in order to compare with the expression of the Radon–Nikodym derivative in that lemma and thus conclude.
We deal with this issue as follows.
Let

\displaystyle\tau_{2\epsilon}:=\inf\big\{t>0\colon\bm{\eta}[0,t]\cap B(0,2\epsilon)\neq\emptyset\big\}

be the first time when
one
of the
n
curves forming the
n
-sided radial
\SLE_{\kappa}
tuple
\bm{\eta}
in common parametrisation hits
\partial B(0,2\epsilon)
.
Given these
n
curves up to time
\tau_{2\epsilon}
, we then concatenate to them
n
additional curves

{\bm{\eta}}^{\bm{w}}_{\epsilon}=(\eta^{\bm{w}}_{1,\epsilon},\ldots,\eta^{\bm{w}}_{n,\epsilon})

which have the law of the inductive construction of Definition
3.3
in the multi-slit domain
\mathbb{D}\setminus\bm{\eta}[0,\tau_{2\epsilon}]
towards 0
,

starting from the tips of
\bm{w}=\bm{\eta}(\tau_{2\epsilon})
, and stopped when they respectively hit
\partial B(0,\epsilon)
.
For each
j
, we denote by
\tilde{\eta}_{j}[0,\tilde{T}_{j,\epsilon}]
the concatenation of
\eta_{j}[0,\tau_{2\epsilon}]
with
\eta^{\bm{w}}_{j,\epsilon}[0,T_{j,\epsilon}]
.
Altogether, this defines
n
curves run until they each hit the ball
B(0,\epsilon)
, which we view as curves up to time-reparametrisation.
We will consider the Radon–Nikodym derivative

\mathrm{d}\tilde{\bm{\eta}}[\bm{0},\tilde{\bm{T}}_{\epsilon}]/\mathrm{d}\bm{\varpi}[\bm{0},\tilde{\bm{T}}_{\epsilon}]
.
Note that by the domain Markov property,
conditional on
\bm{\varpi}[0,\tau_{2\epsilon}]
, the law of each
\varpi_{j}[0,T_{j,\epsilon}]
is that of
the radial
\SLE_{\kappa}
curve
\varpi_{j,\epsilon}[0,T_{j,\epsilon}]
in the slit domain
\mathbb{D}\setminus\varpi_{j}[0,\tau_{2\epsilon}]
towards
0
.
In order to apply Lemma
3.5
, we need to consider independent radial
\SLE_{\kappa}
curves

{\bm{\varpi}}^{\bm{w}}_{\epsilon}:=(\varpi^{\bm{w}}_{1,\epsilon},\ldots,\varpi^{\bm{w}}_{n,\epsilon})

in the multi-slit domain
\mathbb{D}\setminus\bm{\varpi}[0,\tau_{2\epsilon}]
towards
0
,
starting from the tips
\bm{w}=\bm{\varpi}(\tau_{2\epsilon})
, and stopped when they respectively hit
\partial B(0,\epsilon)
.
We have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}\,\tilde{\bm{\eta}}[\bm{0},\tilde{\bm{T}}_{\epsilon}]}{\mathrm{d}\,\bm{\varpi}[\bm{0},\tilde{\bm{T}}_{\epsilon}]}=\;

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}\,\bm{\eta}[0,\tau_{2\epsilon}]}{\mathrm{d}\,\bm{\varpi}[0,\tau_{2\epsilon}]}\;\frac{\mathrm{d}\,{\bm{\eta}}^{\bm{w}}_{\epsilon}[\bm{0},\bm{T}_{\epsilon}]}{\mathrm{d}\,{\bm{\varpi}}^{\bm{w}}_{\epsilon}[\bm{0},\bm{T}_{\epsilon}]}\;\prod_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\mathrm{d}\,\varpi^{\bm{w}}_{j,\epsilon}[0,T_{j,\epsilon}]}{\mathrm{d}\,\varpi_{j,\epsilon}[0,T_{j,\epsilon}]}

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle R(\mathbb{D};\bm{\varpi}[0,\tau_{2\epsilon}])\;R(\mathbb{D}\setminus\bm{\varpi}[0,\tau_{2\epsilon}];{\bm{\varpi}}^{\bm{w}}_{\epsilon}[\bm{0},\bm{T}_{\epsilon}])\;\prod_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\mathrm{d}\,\varpi^{\bm{w}}_{j,\epsilon}[0,T_{j,\epsilon}]}{\mathrm{d}\,\varpi_{j,\epsilon}[0,T_{j,\epsilon}]},

(3.32)

using
[
HL21
, Proposition 3.4 & 3.6]
for the first term and Lemma
3.5
for the second term.
To analyse the third term, consider the following conformal isomorphisms preserving the origin and with positive derivative there:

•

\psi_{\bm{\varpi}[0,\tau_{2\epsilon}]}\colon\mathbb{D}\setminus\bm{\varpi}[0,\tau_{2\epsilon}]\to\mathbb{D}
;

•

\psi_{\varpi_{j}[0,\tau_{2\epsilon}]}\colon\mathbb{D}\setminus\varpi_{j}[0,\tau_{2\epsilon}]\to\mathbb{D}
, for
1\leq j\leq n
;

•

\psi_{j}\colon\mathbb{D}\setminus(\varpi_{j}[0,\tau_{2\epsilon}]\cup\varpi^{\bm{w}}_{j}[0,T_{j,\epsilon}])\to\mathbb{D}
, for
1\leq j\leq n
;

•

\hat{\psi}_{j}\colon\mathbb{D}\setminus(\bm{\varpi}[0,\tau_{2\epsilon}]\cup\varpi^{\bm{w}}_{j}[0,T_{j,\epsilon}])\to\mathbb{D}
, for
1\leq j\leq n
.

Then, by definition of
R
in Equation (
3.5
), we find that

\displaystyle\frac{R(\mathbb{D};\bm{\varpi}[0,\tilde{\bm{T}}_{\epsilon}])}{R(\mathbb{D};\bm{\varpi}[0,\tau_{2\epsilon}])\;R(\mathbb{D}\setminus\bm{\varpi}[0,\tau_{2\epsilon}];\bm{\varpi}^{\bm{w}}_{\epsilon}[\bm{0},\bm{T}_{\epsilon}])}

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\mathbb{1}{\{I_{n}^{\epsilon}\}}\;\prod_{j=1}^{n}\bigg(\frac{(\hat{\psi}_{j}\circ\psi_{j}^{-1})^{\prime}(\psi_{j}(\varpi^{\bm{w}}_{j}(T_{j,\epsilon})))}{(\psi_{\bm{\varpi}[0,\tau_{2\epsilon}]}\circ\psi_{\varpi_{j}[0,\tau_{2\epsilon}]}^{-1})^{\prime}(\psi_{\varpi_{j}[0,\tau_{2\epsilon}]}(\varpi_{j}(\tau_{2\epsilon})))}\bigg)^{\frac{6-\kappa}{2\kappa}}\;\prod_{j=1}^{n}\bigg(\frac{\hat{\psi}_{j}^{\prime}(0)/\psi_{j}^{\prime}(0)}{\psi^{\prime}_{\bm{\varpi}[0,\tau_{2\epsilon}]}(0)/\psi_{\varpi_{j}[0,\tau_{2\epsilon}]}^{\prime}(0)}\bigg)^{\frac{(6-\kappa)(\kappa-2)}{8\kappa}}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{j=1}^{n}\exp\bigg(\frac{(3\kappa-8)(6-\kappa)}{4\kappa}\;\mu_{\mathbb{D}\setminus\bm{\varpi}[0,\tau_{2\epsilon}]}\Big[\ell\cap\varpi_{j}^{\bm{w}}[0,T_{j,\epsilon}]\neq\emptyset\textnormal{ and }\ell\cap\bm{\varpi}[0,\tau_{2\epsilon}]\neq\emptyset\Big]\bigg).

(3.33)

Thus, by
[
HL21
, Proposition 2.2]
and (
3.33
), we have

\displaystyle R(\mathbb{D};\bm{\varpi}[0,\tau_{2\epsilon}])\;R(\mathbb{D}\setminus\bm{\varpi}[0,\tau_{2\epsilon}];{\bm{\varpi}}^{\bm{w}}_{\epsilon}[\bm{0},\bm{T}_{\epsilon}])\;\prod_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\mathrm{d}\,\varpi^{\bm{w}}_{j,\epsilon}[0,T_{j,\epsilon}]}{\mathrm{d}\,\varpi_{j,\epsilon}[0,T_{j,\epsilon}]}=R(\mathbb{D};\bm{\varpi}[0,\tilde{\bm{T}}_{\epsilon}]).

Combining with (
3.32
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}\,\tilde{\bm{\eta}}[\bm{0},\tilde{\bm{T}}_{\epsilon}]}{\mathrm{d}\,\bm{\varpi}[\bm{0},\tilde{\bm{T}}_{\epsilon}]}=R(\mathbb{D};\bm{\varpi}[\bm{0},\tilde{\bm{T}}_{\epsilon}])=\frac{\mathrm{d}\,{\bm{\eta}}^{\bm{z}}[\bm{0},\tilde{\bm{T}}_{\epsilon}]}{\mathrm{d}\bm{\varpi}[\bm{0},\tilde{\bm{T}}_{\epsilon}]},

which implies that
\tilde{\bm{\eta}}[\bm{0},\tilde{\bm{T}}_{\epsilon}]
has the same law as
{\bm{\eta}}^{\bm{z}}[\bm{0},\tilde{\bm{T}}_{\epsilon}]
.
By construction, considering
\tilde{\bm{\eta}}
with the common parametrisation, we see that the law of
(\hat{\eta}^{\bm{z}}_{1,\epsilon},\ldots,\hat{\eta}^{\bm{z}}_{n,\epsilon})
is that of the
n
-sided radial
\SLE_{\kappa}
, as desired.
∎

Now, we finish the proof of Theorem
1.2
.

Proof of Theorem
1.2
.

Lemma
3.6
gives the tightness of
\{(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{1}^{\delta},\ldots,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{n}^{\delta})\}_{\delta>0}
. The convergence after shrinking
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega
to a point is given by Proposition
3.4
and Lemma
3.7
.
∎

3.3
Variance of the winding: proof of Proposition
1.4

Consider the circular Dyson Brownian motion with parameters
\kappa>0
and
\beta\geq 1
,
that is, the unique strong solution
(\Theta_{1},\ldots,\Theta_{n})
to the SDE system

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\Theta_{i}(t)=\sqrt{\kappa}\,\mathrm{d}W_{i}(t)+\frac{\beta\kappa}{4}\sum_{j\neq i}\cot\bigg(\frac{\Theta_{i}(t)-\Theta_{j}(t)}{2}\bigg)\mathrm{d}t,\qquad 1\leq i\leq n,

(3.34)

where
(W_{i}(t)\colon t\geq 0)
for
1\leq i\leq n
are
n
independent Brownian motions on
\mathbb{R}
.
(The existence of a unique strong solution for any
\beta\geq 1
follows from the analogous result for Dyson Brownian motion
[
AGZ10
]
).
First, we consider the asymptotics of the variance of this Dyson Brownian motion.

Lemma 3.8
.

For any
\kappa>0
and
\beta\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{1}{t}\,\mathrm{Var}\big[\Theta_{i}(t)\big]=\frac{\kappa}{n},\qquad 1\leq i\leq n.

(3.35)

Proof.

Let us first consider the case of
i=1
.
The Dyson SDEs (
3.34
) yield

\displaystyle\Sigma(t):=\;

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\Theta_{j}(t)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\qquad\mathrm{d}\Sigma(t)=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathrm{d}\Theta_{j}(t)=\sqrt{\kappa}\,\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathrm{d}W_{j}(t),

(3.36)

\displaystyle\Delta_{j}(t):=\;

\displaystyle\Theta_{j}(t)-\Theta_{1}(t)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\qquad\mathrm{d}\Delta_{j}(t)=\sqrt{\kappa}\,\mathrm{d}(W_{j}(t)-W_{1}(t))

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{\beta\kappa}{4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}2\leq l\leq n\\
l\neq j\end{subarray}}\bigg(\cot\bigg(\frac{\Delta_{j}(t)-\Delta_{l}(t)}{2}\bigg)\mathrm{d}t+\cot\bigg(\frac{\Delta_{l}(t)}{2}\bigg)\bigg)\mathrm{d}t

for
2\leq j\leq n
. Since
W_{1},\ldots,W_{n}
are
n
independent Brownian motions, we see that
\Sigma
and

\Delta_{2},\ldots,\Delta_{n}
are independent. In particular, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\Big[\Sigma(t)-\sum_{j=2}^{n}\Delta_{j}(t)\Big]=\mathrm{Var}[\Sigma(t)]+\mathrm{Var}\Big[\sum_{j=2}^{n}\Delta_{j}(t)\Big]=\kappa nt+\mathrm{Var}\Big[\sum_{j=2}^{n}\Delta_{j}(t)\Big],

(3.37)

where
\mathrm{Var}[\Sigma(t)]=\kappa nt
by (
3.36
).
On the one hand, note that
\Delta_{j}(t)=\Theta_{j}(t)-\Theta_{1}(t)\in[0,2\pi]
for every
t\geq 0
and for
2\leq j\leq n
.
Hence, there exists a constant
M>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\Big[\sum_{j=2}^{n}\Delta_{j}(t)\Big]\leq Mn^{2}.

(3.38)

On the other hand, note that

\displaystyle\Theta_{1}(t)=\frac{1}{n}\Big(\Sigma(t)-\sum_{j=2}^{n}\Delta_{j}(t)\Big).

Combining this with (
3.37
,
3.38
), we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{1}{t}\,\mathrm{Var}\big[\Theta_{i}(t)\big]=\frac{\kappa}{n}.

(3.39)

Lastly, as
\Theta_{j}(t)=\Delta_{j}(t)+\Theta_{1}(t)
for
2\leq j\leq n
, Equation (
3.39
) also yields (
3.35
) for
j\neq 1
.
∎

For each
n\geq 1
, we sample the starting points
(\Theta_{1}(0),\ldots,\Theta_{n}(0))=(\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{n})
from the density

\displaystyle\rho(\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{n})=\frac{1}{\mathcal{Z}_{n}}\prod_{1\leq i\leq j\leq n}\sin\bigg(\frac{\theta_{j}-\theta_{i}}{2}\bigg).

Then, if
(\Theta_{1}(t),\ldots,\Theta_{n}(t))
for
t\geq 0
evolves according to (
3.34
), then

\big((\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta_{1}(t)},\ldots,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta_{n}(t)})\colon t\geq 0\big)
is a stationary Markov process on
\mathcal{X}_{n}
.
Now, we are ready to prove Proposition
1.4
.
The proof is very similar to the proof of
[
Sch00
, Equation (7.3)]
.
We now take
\kappa=2
and
\beta=4
.

Proof of Proposition
1.4
.

Let
\bm{\eta}
be a
n
-sided radial
\SLE_{2}
, whose Loewner driving process as in (
3.14
)
is given by the circular Dyson Brownian motion with parameters
\kappa=2
and
\beta=4
in (
3.34
).
Fix
T>0
and for
0\leq t\leq T
, define
h_{t}(w):=g_{T-t}(g_{T}^{-1}(w))
for
w\in\mathbb{S}
,
so that
h_{t}(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}W_{j}(T)}):=g_{T-t}(\eta_{j}(T))
, for
1\leq j\leq n
.
We can write
h_{t}(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}W_{j}(T)})=\exp(X_{j}(t)+\mathrm{i}Y_{j}(t))
, where

(X_{j}(t)\colon 0<t\leq T)
and
(Y_{j}(t)\colon 0<t\leq T)
satisfy

\displaystyle\partial_{t}X_{j}(t)+\mathrm{i}\partial_{t}Y_{j}(t)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\sinh(X_{j}(t))+\mathrm{i}\sin\big(\Theta_{i}(T-t)-Y_{j}(t)\big)}{\cosh(X_{j}(t))-\cos\big(\Theta_{i}(T-t)-Y_{j}(t)\big)},\qquad 1\leq j\leq n.

(3.40)

Like in the proof of
[
Sch00
, Theorem 7.2]
,
because
\partial_{t}X_{j}(t)<0
for
0<t\leq T
, we can write
Y_{j}
as a function of
X_{j}
, say
Y_{j}=F(X_{j})
. By (
3.40
), we have

\displaystyle|F^{\prime}(X_{j}(t))|\leq\frac{n}{|\sinh X_{j}(t)|}.

Now, let
T_{j}
to be the first time when
X_{j}=-1
.
Then, as in
[
Sch00
, Equation (7.7)]
, we find that

\displaystyle|Y_{j}(T)-Y_{j}(T_{j})|\lesssim\int_{-\infty}^{-1}\frac{\mathrm{d}x}{|\sinh x|}<\infty.

(3.41)

Now, set
\psi_{j}(s,t)=g_{T-t}(\eta_{j}(T-s))
for
0\leq s\leq t\leq T
. Note that the image of
\psi_{j}
does not contain
0
and
\psi_{j}
is a homotopy between
(\psi_{j}(s,T)\colon 0\leq s\leq T)
and
(\psi_{j}(0,T-t)\colon 0\leq t\leq T)\cup(\psi_{j}(t,t)\colon 0\leq t\leq T)
.
Thus, we can compute the topological winding
\varphi_{j}(T)
of
\eta_{j}[0,T]
around the origin as

\displaystyle\varphi_{j}(T):=\arg\eta_{j}(T)-\arg\eta_{j}(0)=\Theta_{j}(T)-\Theta_{j}(0)+Y_{j}(T)-Y_{j}(T_{j})+Y_{j}(T_{j})-Y_{j}(0).

From (
3.41
) and the proof of Lemma
3.8
,
if
f
is a bounded continuous test function, then we have

\displaystyle\lim_{T\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\Big[f\Big(\frac{\varphi_{1}(T)}{\sqrt{2T/n}},\ldots,\frac{\varphi_{n}(T)}{\sqrt{2T/n}}\Big)\Big]=\lim_{T\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\Big[f\Big(\frac{\Sigma(T)}{\sqrt{2nT}},\ldots,\frac{\Sigma(T)}{\sqrt{2nT}}\Big)\Big]=\lim_{T\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[f(X,\ldots,X)\big],

where
\Sigma(t):=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\Theta_{j}(t)
as in (
3.36
) in the proof of Lemma
3.8
,
and where
X\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
.
∎

4
Flow-line coupling for
n
-sided radial SLE with GFF

Lastly, we derive a connection of the
n
-sided radial
\SLE_{\kappa}
, for
\kappa\in(0,4)
,
with a variant
\Phi
of the Gaussian free field (GFF) having a singularity at the origin with additive monodromy.
Here,
\bm{\eta}
represent “flow lines” of the “ill-defined” vector field of type
\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Phi}
.
The intuition from the dimer height function goes as follows (cf. Figure
1.2
).
While the discrete dimer height function
\mathrm{h}^{\delta}
does define flow lines
\vartheta^{\delta}\colon[0,t]\to\mathbb{C}

by solving the related flow ODE
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\vartheta^{\delta}(t)=\exp\big(\mathrm{i}\mathrm{h}^{\delta}(\vartheta^{\delta}(t))\big)
,
its scaling limit — the GFF — is not a function but rather a random Schwartz distribution.
The theory of flow lines for the GFF was extensively developed by Miller and Sheffield
[
MS16
,
MS17
]
, and earlier by Dubédat
[
Dub09
]
.
The series of works
[
BLR20
,
BLR24
,
BLR25
]

addresses the case of present interest from the point of view of the dimer height function.
We refer to these works for more background and details, and keep this section very concise.

4.1
Gaussian free field

Suppose
D\subset\mathbb{C}
is a regular domain (in the sense, say of
[
BP25
]
, so that the Dirichlet Green’s function is finite).
The
zero-boundary GFF

\mathrm{h}
on
D
is a random distribution such that for every smooth compactly supported test function
f\in C^{\infty}_{c}(D)
on
D
,
the quantity
(\mathrm{h},f)
is a Gaussian random variable with mean zero and variance obtained from the Green’s function
\mathrm{G}_{D}
on
D
with Dirichlet boundary condition:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\mathrm{h},f)]=0\qquad\textnormal{and}\qquad\mathbb{E}[(\mathrm{h},f)^{2}]=\int_{D}\int_{D}f(z)\,\mathrm{G}_{D}(g_{t}(z),g_{t}(w))\,f(w)\;\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}w.

Here, we normalise the Dirichlet Green’s function so that
\mathrm{G}_{D}(z,w)=-\log|z-w|+O(1)
as
|z-w|\to 0
.
The GFF with boundary data
u
is the sum of the zero-boundary GFF on
D
and harmonic extension of
u
to
D
.
See
[
BP25
]
for a more detailed introduction.

In
[
MS17
]
, the authors establish the theory of
flow lines
for the Gaussian free field with a singularity at one interior point.
The procedure goes roughly as follows. The GFF can be coupled with a radial
\SLE_{\kappa}(\rho)
process by taking the field to be
\mathrm{h}+\alpha\arg(\cdot)
on
\mathbb{D}
, where
\alpha=\alpha(\rho)\in\mathbb{R}
is the strength of the singularity at the origin
and one assumes certain boundary data on
\partial\mathbb{D}
depending on
\kappa

[
MS17
, Proposition 3.1]
.
In the present article, we consider a similar setting:
we will derive a coupling of the
n
-sided
\SLE_{\kappa}
curves
\bm{\eta}=(\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{n})

with a Gaussian free field having suitable boundary data and singularity at the origin, yielding Theorem
1.5
.
Although the value of
\kappa=2
is the most natural in the context of the uniform spanning tree,
with the same effort we may consider arbitrary
\kappa\in(0,4)
.
The (critical) case of
\kappa=4
would correspond to a level-line with monodromy
[
Dub09
]
, which we shall not consider here.

4.2
GFF flow lines with additive monodromy: proof of Theorem
1.5

Let us first recall the ingredients of the claim in Theorem
1.5
.
Fix
\kappa\in(0,4)
and
\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\bm{\theta}}\in\mathcal{X}_{n}
, and set

\displaystyle\chi_{\kappa}:=\frac{2}{\sqrt{\kappa}}-\frac{\sqrt{\kappa}}{2}\qquad\textnormal{and}\qquad\lambda_{\kappa}:=\frac{\pi}{\sqrt{\kappa}}.

Consider the harmonic function
\,\mathfrak{h}\colon\mathbb{D}\to\mathbb{R}
defined for
w\in\mathbb{D}
as in (
1.10
):

\displaystyle\,\mathfrak{h}(w)=\,\mathfrak{h}_{\bm{\theta}}(w):=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\kappa}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\arg\bigg(\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\theta_{j}}-w}{1-\overline{w}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\theta_{j}}}\bigg)=-\frac{2}{\sqrt{\kappa}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\arg(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\theta_{j}}-w)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\kappa}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\theta_{j}.

Let
\mathrm{h}
be a GFF field with Dirichlet boundary conditions in
\mathbb{D}
, and define the field as in (
1.11
),

\displaystyle\Phi(\cdot):=\mathrm{h}(\cdot)+\Big(\chi_{\kappa}+\frac{n}{\sqrt{\kappa}}\Big)\arg(\cdot)+\,\mathfrak{h}_{\bm{\theta}}(\cdot),

having additive monodromy at the origin due to the multivalued function
\arg(\cdot)
.
We consider flow lines
\bm{\vartheta}=(\vartheta_{1},\ldots,\vartheta_{n})
of the formal vector field of type
\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Phi}
, starting from
\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\bm{\theta}}
and with respective angles

\displaystyle\alpha_{j}=\frac{2\lambda_{\kappa}(j-1)}{\chi_{\kappa}},\qquad 1\leq j\leq n.

These are random curves in the space
X_{0}^{n}
(with metric (
3.20
)) coupled with
\Phi
so that for all stopping times
\bm{\tau}=(\tau_{1},\ldots,\tau_{n})
,
conditionally given
\bm{\vartheta}[0,\bm{\tau}]=(\vartheta_{1}[0,\tau_{1}],\ldots,\vartheta_{n}[0,\tau_{n}])
, we have

\displaystyle\Phi|_{\mathbb{D}_{\bm{\tau}}}=\tilde{\Phi}\circ g_{\bm{\tau}}-\chi_{\kappa}\arg g^{\prime}_{\bm{\tau}},

where
\tilde{\Phi}
has the same law as (
1.11
), and
g_{\bm{\tau}}\colon\mathbb{D}_{\bm{\tau}}\to\mathbb{D}
is the unique conformal isomorphism uniformising the complement of
\mathbb{D}_{\bm{\tau}}:=\mathbb{D}\setminus\bm{\vartheta}[0,\bm{\tau}]

normalised such that
g_{\bm{\tau}}(0)=0
and
g^{\prime}_{\bm{\tau}}(0)>0
.

We claim that, after parameterising
\bm{\vartheta}
with the common (capacity) parametrisation, their law agrees with that of the
n
-sided radial
\SLE_{\kappa}
curves
\bm{\eta}
,
whose Loewner driving process is the circular Dyson Brownian motion with parameters
\kappa=2
and
\beta=4
as in (
1.9
).
In essence, this is encapsulated in the asserted Equation (
1.12
) in Theorem
1.5
, which we now proceed to prove.

Proof of Theorem
1.5
.

The strategy is same as the proof of
[
MS16
, Theorem 1.1]
and
[
MS17
, Proposition 3.1]
. Fix
\epsilon>0
and write
\bm{z}=\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\bm{\theta}}
.
As in Lemma
3.7
, the law of the curves
(\hat{\eta}^{\bm{z}}_{1,\epsilon},\ldots,\hat{\eta}^{\bm{z}}_{n,\epsilon})
with common parametrisation is that of the
n
-sided radial
\SLE_{\kappa}
curves
(\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{n})
,
up to the hitting time
\tau_{\epsilon}
to
\partial B(0,\epsilon)
.
Let
(\eta^{\bm{z}}_{1,\epsilon},\ldots,\eta^{\bm{z}}_{n,\epsilon})
be the curves related to
(\hat{\eta}^{\bm{z}}_{1,\epsilon},\ldots,\hat{\eta}^{\bm{z}}_{n,\epsilon})
as in Lemma
3.7
,
let
T_{1,\epsilon}=T<\tau_{\epsilon}
be a stopping time for the first curve
\eta^{\bm{z}}_{1,\epsilon}
, and let
T_{j,\epsilon}=0
for all
2\leq j\leq n
.
Let
(\Theta(t)\colon 0\leq t\leq T)
be the Loewner driving function of
\eta^{\bm{z}}_{1,\epsilon}
,
and
(g_{t}\colon 0\leq t\leq T)
the associated radial Loewner flow (
3.18
).
Also, let
\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}V_{j}(t)}=g_{t}(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\theta_{j}})
be the time-evolutions of the force points of
\eta^{\bm{z}}_{1,\epsilon}
, for
2\leq j\leq n
.

By considering a partition of unity, for the GFF we only need to consider test functions
f
whose support is simply connected and does not contain
0
.
Let
\mathrm{G}_{\mathbb{D}}(\cdot,\cdot)
denote the Green’s function on
\mathbb{D}
with Dirichlet boundary condition.
For fixed
a\in\mathbb{R}
, consider the process

\displaystyle M(t):=\;

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\exp\Big(\mathrm{i}a\Big(\mathrm{h}\circ g_{t}(\cdot)+\big(\chi_{\kappa}+\tfrac{n}{\sqrt{\kappa}}\big)\arg g_{t}(\cdot)+\,\mathfrak{h}_{\Theta(t),V_{2}(t),\ldots,V_{n}(t)}(g_{t}(\cdot))-\chi_{\kappa}\arg g^{\prime}_{t}(\cdot)\Big),\;f(\cdot)\Big)\;\Big|\;\eta_{1}[0,t]\bigg]

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\exp\Big(\mathrm{i}a\Big(\big(\chi_{\kappa}+\tfrac{n}{\sqrt{\kappa}}\big)\arg g_{t}(\cdot)+\,\mathfrak{h}_{\Theta(t),V_{2}(t),\ldots,V_{n}(t)}(g_{t}(\cdot))-\chi_{\kappa}\arg g^{\prime}_{t}(\cdot)\Big),\;f(\cdot)\Big)

\displaystyle\times\exp\Big(\!-\frac{a^{2}}{2}\int_{\mathbb{D}}\int_{\mathbb{D}}f(z)\,\mathrm{G}_{\mathbb{D}}(g_{t}(z),g_{t}(w))\,f(w)\;\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}w\Big),\qquad 0\leq t\leq T.

To establish the coupling, it suffices to show that
(M(t\wedge T)\colon t\geq 0)
is a martingale.
By Itô’s formula and Fubini’s theorem, this is equivalent to verifying that for all
z,w\in\mathbb{D}
,
the process
(H(t\wedge T)\colon t\geq 0)
,

\displaystyle H(t):=\big(\chi_{\kappa}+\tfrac{n}{\sqrt{\kappa}}\big)\arg g_{t}(z)+\,\mathfrak{h}_{\Theta(t),V_{2}(t),\ldots,V_{n}(t)}(g_{t}(z))-\chi_{\kappa}\arg g^{\prime}_{t}(z),\qquad 0\leq t\leq T,

is a martingale, and the quadratic variation of the harmonic function agrees with the Hadamard formula

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\big\langle\,\mathfrak{h}_{\Theta(t),V_{2}(t),\ldots,V_{n}(t)}(g_{t}(z)),\;\,\mathfrak{h}_{\Theta(t),V_{2}(t),\ldots,V_{n}(t)}(g_{t}(w))\big\rangle=-\mathrm{d}\mathrm{G}_{\mathbb{D}}(g_{t}(z),g_{t}(w)).

(4.1)

Let
W
be the Brownian motion in the SDE (
3.16
) for
\Theta
.
By a direct computation, we find

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\arg\big(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}-g_{t}(z)\big)=\sqrt{\kappa}\;\mathrm{Re}\bigg(\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}}{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}-g_{t}(z)}\bigg)\mathrm{d}W(t)

\displaystyle+\;

\displaystyle\bigg(\mathrm{Re}\bigg(\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}}{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}-g_{t}(z)}\sum_{j=2}^{n}\cot\bigg(\frac{\Theta(t)-V_{j}(t)}{2}\bigg)\bigg)-\mathrm{Im}\bigg(\frac{g_{t}(z)}{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}-g_{t}(z)}\bigg)^{2}\!\!+\bigg(\frac{\kappa}{2}-1\bigg)\mathrm{Im}\bigg(\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}g_{t}(z)}{(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}-g_{t}(z))^{2}}\bigg)\bigg)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\sqrt{\kappa}\;\mathrm{Re}\bigg(\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}}{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}-g_{t}(z)}\bigg)\mathrm{d}W(t)-\bigg(\sum_{j=2}^{n}\mathrm{Im}\bigg(\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}+\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}V_{j}(t)})}{(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}-g_{t}(z))(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}-\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}V_{j}(t)})}\bigg)

(4.2)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\mathrm{Im}\bigg(\frac{g_{t}(z)}{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}-g_{t}(z)}\bigg)^{2}\!\!-\bigg(\frac{\kappa}{2}-1\bigg)\mathrm{Im}\bigg(\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}g_{t}(z)}{(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}-g_{t}(z))^{2}}\bigg)\bigg)\mathrm{d}t,

and for
2\leq j\leq n
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\arg\big(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}V_{j}(t)}-g_{t}(z)\big)=2\,\mathrm{Im}\bigg(\frac{e^{2\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}}{(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}-g_{t}(z))(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}-\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}V_{j}(t)})}\bigg)\mathrm{d}t.

(4.3)

Moreover, from the Loewner equation (
3.18
) we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\arg g_{t}(z)=\;

\displaystyle\mathrm{Im}\bigg(\frac{2\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}}{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}-g_{t}(z)}\bigg)\mathrm{d}t,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\arg g^{\prime}_{t}(z)=\;

\displaystyle\bigg(\mathrm{Im}\bigg(\frac{2\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}}{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}-g_{t}(z)}\bigg)+\mathrm{Im}\bigg(\frac{2\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}g_{t}(z)}{(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}-g_{t}(z))^{2}}\bigg)\bigg)\mathrm{d}t,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\Big(\Theta(t)+\sum_{j=2}^{n}V_{j}(t)\Big)=\;

\displaystyle\sqrt{\kappa}\,\mathrm{d}W(t).

(4.4)

Combining (
4.2
)–(
4.4
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}H(t)=\bigg(\!\!-2\mathrm{Re}\bigg(\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}}{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}-g_{t}(z)}\bigg)+1\bigg)\mathrm{d}W(t),

(4.5)

which proves that the process
(H(t\wedge T)\colon t\geq 0)
is a martingale.
To get the identity (
4.1
), we can use the explicit formula

\displaystyle\mathrm{G}_{\mathbb{D}}(g_{t}(z),g_{t}(w))=-\log\bigg|\frac{g_{t}(z)-g_{t}(w)}{1-\overline{g_{t}(z)}g_{t}(w)}\bigg|,

to obtain

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\mathrm{G}_{\mathbb{D}}(g_{t}(z),g_{t}(w))=1-2\,\mathrm{Re}\bigg(\frac{\mathrm{e}^{2\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}}{(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}-g_{t}(z))(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}-g_{t}(w))}+\frac{\overline{g_{t}(z)}g_{t}(w)}{(e^{-i\Theta(t)}-\overline{g_{t}(z)})(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}-g_{t}(w))}\bigg).

Writing
a:=\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}}{(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}-g_{t}(z))}
and
b:=\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}}{(\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\Theta(t)}-g_{t}(w))}
, we see that

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\mathrm{G}_{\mathbb{D}}(g_{t}(z),g_{t}(w))=1-2\,\mathrm{Re}\big(ab+\overline{(1-a)}(1-b)\big)=1-4\,(\mathrm{Re}\,a)(\mathrm{Re}\,b)+2\,\mathrm{Re}\,a+2\,\mathrm{Re}\,b,

which, combined with (
4.5
), implies (
4.1
) and completes the proof.
∎

Appendix A
Brownian loop identity via martingale argument

The below Lemma
A.1
is a re-statement of Equation (
3.9
) in Proposition
3.2
, which we prove here using scaling limit results and a martingale argument.

Lemma A.1
.

Suppose
\varrho
is a simple curve on
\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}}}
which only intersects
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\Omega
at its starting point and does not hit
0
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\diam(\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\Omega)\to 0}\exp\Big(2\mu_{\Omega}^{\textnormal{ND}}\big[\ell\in\mathcal{L}_{*}\textnormal{ such that }\tfrac{1}{2\pi}\varphi(\ell)\textnormal{ is odd and }\ell\cap\varrho\neq\emptyset\big]\Big)=\bigg|\frac{\phi_{\varrho}^{\prime}(0)}{\phi^{\prime}(0)}\bigg|^{1/4},

(A.1)

where
\phi\colon\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}}}\to\mathbb{D}
and
\phi_{\varrho}\colon\Omega_{\scalebox{0.6}{{fill}}}\setminus\varrho\to\mathbb{D}
are any conformal isomorphisms fixing the origin.

Proof.

By Equation (
3.13
) and the conformal invariance of Brownian loop measure, it suffices to prove (
A.1
) when
\Omega=\mathbb{A}_{r}
.
In this case, we can take
\phi
to be the identity map and
c=c(\varrho):=\phi_{\varrho}^{\prime}(0)>1
.
Note that the right-hand side of (
3.13
) only depends on the distortion factor
c
:

\displaystyle\mu_{\mathbb{D}}\big[\ell\in\mathcal{L}_{*}\textnormal{ such that }\tfrac{1}{2\pi}\varphi(\ell)\textnormal{ is odd and }\ell\cap\varrho\neq\emptyset\big]

(A.2)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\lim_{r\to 0}\bigg(\mu_{\mathbb{A}_{r}}^{\textnormal{ND}}\big[\ell\in\mathcal{L}_{*}\textnormal{ such that }\tfrac{1}{2\pi}\varphi(\ell)\textnormal{ is odd }\big]\,-\,\mu_{\mathbb{A}_{r}\setminus\varrho}^{\textnormal{ND}}\big[\ell\in\mathcal{L}_{*}\textnormal{ such that }\tfrac{1}{2\pi}\varphi(\ell)\textnormal{ is odd }\big]\bigg)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\lim_{r\to 0}\bigg(\mu_{\mathbb{A}_{r}}^{\textnormal{ND}}\big[\ell\in\mathcal{L}_{*}\textnormal{ such that }\tfrac{1}{2\pi}\varphi(\ell)\textnormal{ is odd }\big]\,-\,\mu_{\mathbb{A}_{cr}}^{\textnormal{ND}}\big[\ell\in\mathcal{L}_{*}\textnormal{ such that }\tfrac{1}{2\pi}\varphi(\ell)\textnormal{ is odd }\big]\bigg)

[conf. inv.]

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\mu_{\mathbb{A}_{1/c}}^{\textnormal{ND}}\big[\ell\in\mathcal{L}_{*}\textnormal{ such that }\tfrac{1}{2\pi}\varphi(\ell)\textnormal{ is odd }\big]\,=:\,F(c).

[conf. inv.]

As in the proof of Proposition
3.4
when
n=1
,
we will denote by
\Gamma_{w}^{\delta}
the loop-erasure of a random walk
\mathcal{R}\sim\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{w}
on
\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}
starting from
w^{\delta}\in\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}
and ending at
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}
, reflected at
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{in}}}\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}
;
and by
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{w}^{\delta}
its time-reversal.
Next, we will use a martingale technique to obtain the explicit formula (
A.1
).
Consider the case
n=2
and take
\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}:=\mathbb{A}_{r}\cap\delta\mathbb{Z}^{2}
.
We take
\alpha_{2}^{\delta}
to be an edge
e^{\delta}
and assume that it converges to
e\in\partial\mathbb{D}
as
\delta\to 0
;
and we take
x_{1}=r
and
x_{2}=-r
.
Recall the definition of
E^{\delta}_{\bm{x},\bm{\alpha}}=E(\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta};x_{1}^{\delta},x_{2}^{\delta};\alpha_{1}^{\delta},\alpha_{2}^{\delta})
in Equation (
3.1
) for the UST branches.
For every discrete simple curve
\varrho^{\delta}
which intersects
\alpha_{1}^{\delta}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\delta}\Big[\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{1}^{\delta}[0,t\wedge\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}]=\varrho^{\delta}\;\Big|\;E(\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta};x_{1}^{\delta},x_{2}^{\delta};\alpha_{1}^{\delta},\alpha_{2}^{\delta})\Big]

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{P}^{\delta}\big[E(\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}\cup\varrho^{\delta};x_{1}^{\delta},x_{2}^{\delta};\varrho^{\delta}(t\wedge\tau_{\epsilon}^{\delta}),\alpha_{2}^{\delta})\big]}{\mathbb{P}^{\delta}\big[E(\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta};x_{1}^{\delta},x_{2}^{\delta};\alpha_{1}^{\delta},\alpha_{2}^{\delta})\big]}\;\frac{\#\{\textnormal{spanning trees $\mathcal{T}^{\delta}$ on }\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}\textnormal{ s.t. }\varrho^{\delta}\subset\mathcal{T}^{\delta}\}}{\#\{\textnormal{spanning trees $\mathcal{T}^{\delta}$ on }\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}\}}

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{P}^{\delta}\big[E(\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}\cup\varrho^{\delta};x_{1}^{\delta},x_{2}^{\delta};\varrho^{\delta}(t\wedge\tau_{\epsilon}^{\delta}),\alpha_{2}^{\delta})\big]}{\mathbb{P}^{\delta}\big[E(\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta};x_{1}^{\delta},x_{2}^{\delta};\alpha_{1}^{\delta},\alpha_{2}^{\delta})\big]}\;\frac{\mathbb{P}^{\delta}\big[\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{x_{1}}^{\delta}[0,t\wedge\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}]=\varrho^{\delta}\big]}{\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{x_{1}}[\mathcal{R}\textnormal{ hits }\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}\cup\varrho^{\delta}\textnormal{ at }\varrho^{\delta}(t\wedge\tau_{\epsilon}^{\delta})]},

where we denote by
\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}\cup\varrho^{\delta}
the graph obtained by wiring
\varrho^{\delta}
and
\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}
together.
Thus, the Radon–Nikodym derivative of the conditional law of the time-reversed branch
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{1}^{\delta}[0,t\wedge\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}]
given
E(\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta};x_{1}^{\delta},x_{2}^{\delta};\alpha_{1}^{\delta},\alpha_{2}^{\delta})
, with respect to the time-reversed LERW
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{x_{1}}^{\delta}[0,t\wedge\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}]
, is given by

\displaystyle M(t\wedge\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}):=\frac{\mathbb{1}{\{\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{x_{1}}^{\delta}(0)\in\alpha_{1}^{\delta}\}}}{\mathbb{P}^{\delta}\big[E(\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta};x_{1}^{\delta},x_{2}^{\delta};\alpha_{1}^{\delta},\alpha_{2}^{\delta})\big]}\;\frac{\mathbb{P}^{\delta}\big[E(\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}\cup\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{x_{1}}^{\delta}[0,t\wedge\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}];x_{1}^{\delta},x_{2}^{\delta};\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{x_{1}}^{\delta}(t\wedge\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}),\alpha_{2}^{\delta})\big]}{\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{x_{1}}[\mathcal{R}\textnormal{ hits }\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}\cup\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{x_{1}}^{\delta}[0,t\wedge\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}]\textnormal{ at }\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{x_{1}}^{\delta}(t\wedge\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta})]}.

In particular,
(M(t\wedge\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta})\colon t\geq 0)
is a discrete martingale for
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{x_{1}}^{\delta}
.
Define

\displaystyle N(t\wedge\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}):=\;

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{P}^{\delta}\big[E(\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}\cup\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{x_{1}}^{\delta}[0,t\wedge\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}];x_{1}^{\delta},x_{2}^{\delta};\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{x_{1}}^{\delta}(t\wedge\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}),\alpha_{2}^{\delta})\big]}{\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{x_{1}}[\mathcal{R}\textnormal{ hits }\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}\cup\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{x_{1}}^{\delta}[0,t\wedge\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}]\textnormal{ at }\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{x_{1}}^{\delta}(t\wedge\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta})]\;\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{x_{2}}[\mathcal{R}\textnormal{ hits }\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}\cup\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{x_{1}}^{\delta}[0,t\wedge\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}]\textnormal{ through }\alpha_{2}^{\delta}]}

\displaystyle\times\frac{\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{x_{2}}[\mathcal{R}\textnormal{ hits }\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}\cup\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{x_{1}}^{\delta}[0,t\wedge\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}]\textnormal{ through }\alpha_{2}^{\delta}]}{\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{x_{2}}[\mathcal{R}\textnormal{ hits }\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}\textnormal{ through }\alpha_{2}^{\delta}]}\;\mathbb{1}{\{\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{z}^{\delta}(0)\in\alpha_{1}^{\delta}\}}

Then,
N=(N(t\wedge\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta})\colon t\geq 0)
is a discrete martingale for
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{x_{1}}^{\delta}

— and by a Harnack-type inequality, there exists a constant
C=C(\epsilon)>0
that only depends on
\epsilon
, such that

\displaystyle N(t\wedge\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta})\;\leq\;1\;+\;\frac{\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{x_{2}}[\mathcal{R}\textnormal{ hits }\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}\cup\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{x_{1}}^{\delta}[0,t\wedge\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}]\textnormal{ at }\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{x_{1}}^{\delta}(t\wedge\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta})]\;\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{x_{1}}[\mathcal{R}\textnormal{ hits }\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}\textnormal{ through }\alpha_{2}^{\delta}]}{\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{x_{1}}[\mathcal{R}\textnormal{ hits }\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}\cup\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{x_{1}}^{\delta}[0,t\wedge\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta}]\textnormal{ at }\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{x_{1}}^{\delta}(t\wedge\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}^{\delta})]\;\mathsf{P}^{\delta}_{x_{2}}[\mathcal{R}\textnormal{ hits }\partial_{\scalebox{0.6}{{out}}}\mathbb{A}_{r}^{\delta}\textnormal{ through }\alpha_{2}^{\delta}]}\;\leq\;C.

We have proved the convergence (both in
(X,\dist_{X})
and locally uniformly)
of the time-reversed UST branch
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{1}^{\delta}
to the radial
\SLE_{2}
curve
\varpi=\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\gamma$}}$}{}_{1}

in the proof of Proposition
3.4
(case
n=1
), when first
\delta\to 0
and then
r\to 0
.
Similarly, the family
\{\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{x_{1}}^{\delta}\}_{\delta>0}
is tight in
(X,\dist_{X})

(cf.
[
LSW04
, Theorem 3.9]
and
[
BL23
, Lemma 3.2]
).
Suppose
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{r}\in X
is any subsequential limit,
which we parameterise by capacity. It follows similarly as in the proof of
[
LSW04
, Theorem 3.9]

that the family
\{\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{x_{1}}^{\delta}\}_{\delta>0}
is also tight in the uniform topology of curves parameterised by capacity.
Thus, by Skorokhod’s representation theorem, without loss of generality we may assume that along a subsequence still denoted
\delta\to 0
,
the time-reversed LERW
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{x_{1}}^{\delta}
converges almost surely to
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{r}
in both
(X,\dist_{X})
and locally uniformly.

Consider the conformal isomorphism
\phi^{(r)}_{t,\epsilon}\colon\mathbb{A}_{r}\setminus\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{r}[0,t\wedge\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}]\to\mathbb{A}_{r_{t,\epsilon}}
, such that
\phi^{(r)}_{t,\epsilon}(r)=:r({t\wedge\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}})=:r_{t,\epsilon}
.
The arguments leading to Equation (
3.24
) show that

\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{r}[0,\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}]\to\varpi[0,\tau_{\epsilon}]
as
r\to 0
in total variation distance.
In particular, there exists a coupling such that almost surely, for every
t\geq 0
, the curve
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{r}[0,t]
agrees with the radial
\SLE_{2}
curve
\varpi[0,t]
when
r>0
is small enough.
Hence,
\phi^{(r)}_{t,\epsilon}
converges to the radial Loewner flow
g_{t\wedge\tau_{\epsilon}}
corresponding to
\varpi
in (
3.14
) locally uniformly on
\mathbb{D}\setminus(\{0\}\cup\varpi[0,t\wedge\tau_{\epsilon}])
.

Similarly to Lemma
2.12
, one can prove that
N
converges almost surely as
\delta\to 0
to a limit
N_{r}
, which satisfies the following two properties.

•

The process
N_{r}=(N_{r}(t\wedge\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon})\colon t\geq 0)
is a martingale for
\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{r}
,
and we have
N_{r}(t\wedge\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon})\leq C
.

•

Let
\mathbb{A}_{r}(t,\epsilon):=\mathbb{A}_{r}\setminus\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{r}[0,t\wedge\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}]
.
We can write
N
in the form

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636ptN_{r}(t\wedge\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon})=\;

\displaystyle\mathbb{1}{\{\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{r}(0)\in\alpha_{1}\}}\;r_{t,\epsilon}\;\exp\Big(\!-2\mu_{\mathbb{A}_{r}(t,\epsilon)}^{\textnormal{ND}}\big[\ell\in\mathcal{L}_{*}\textnormal{ such that }\tfrac{1}{2\pi}\varphi(\ell)\textnormal{ is odd }\big]\Big)

\displaystyle\times\sin\left(\frac{\arg\phi^{(r)}_{t,\epsilon}(x_{2})-\arg\phi^{(r)}_{t,\epsilon}(x_{1})}{2}\right)\;\sin\left(\frac{\arg\phi^{(r)}_{t,\epsilon}(e)-\arg\phi^{(r)}_{t,\epsilon}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{r}(t\wedge\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}))}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\times\frac{|\big(\phi^{(r)}_{t,\epsilon}\big)^{\prime}(e)|}{\mathrm{H}^{\textnormal{ND}}_{\mathbb{A}_{r}}(x_{2},e)\;\mathrm{H}^{\textnormal{ND}}_{\mathbb{A}_{r_{t,\epsilon}}}(\phi^{(r)}_{t,\epsilon}(x_{1}),\phi^{(r)}_{t,\epsilon}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{r}(t\wedge\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon})))}\;\times(1+O(r_{t,\epsilon})),

(A.3)

where
\mathrm{H}^{\textnormal{ND}}_{\smash{\mathbb{A}_{r}}}(\cdot,\cdot)
is the Poisson kernel on
\smash{\mathbb{A}_{r}}
with Dirichlet boundary condition on
\partial B(0,1)

and Neumann boundary condition on
\partial B(0,r)
: the function
\mathrm{H}^{\textnormal{ND}}_{\smash{\mathbb{A}_{r}}}(\cdot,\cdot)
is smooth on the set

\displaystyle(\overline{\mathbb{A}}_{r}\times\overline{\mathbb{A}}_{r})\setminus\{(y,y)\;|\;y\in\partial B(0,1)\};

for every
z\in\partial B(0,1)
, the function
\mathrm{H}^{\textnormal{ND}}_{\smash{\mathbb{A}_{r}}}(\cdot,y)
is harmonic on
\smash{\mathbb{A}_{r}}
, and

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{\textnormal{ND}}_{\smash{\mathbb{A}_{r}}}(\cdot,y)|_{\partial B(0,1)\setminus\{y\}}=0\qquad\textnormal{and}\qquad\partial_{n}\mathrm{H}^{\textnormal{ND}}_{\smash{\mathbb{A}_{r}}}(\cdot,z)|_{\partial B(0,r)}=0.

Next, for
c>0
, define
\sigma_{c}
to be the first time for the curves such that
r(\sigma)=cr

(we can first take
r
small enough and then take
\epsilon
small enough such that
\sigma_{c}<\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\tau$}}$}{}_{\epsilon}
).
By the optional stopping theorem,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[N_{r}(\sigma_{c})]=\mathbb{E}[N_{r}(0)].

(A.4)

By a Harnack-type inequality, for every
\epsilon_{0}>0
, there exists
s_{0}>0
, such that for every

s<s_{0}
,

\displaystyle\big|\mathrm{H}^{\textnormal{ND}}_{\mathbb{A}_{s}}(x,e)-\tfrac{1}{2\pi}\big|<\epsilon_{0},\qquad x\in\partial B(0,s),\;e\in\partial\mathbb{D}.

(A.5)

Plugging (
A.5
) into (
A.3
,
A.4
), we find that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{c}\;\sin\bigg(\frac{\arg x_{2}-\arg x_{1}}{2}\bigg)\;\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sin\bigg(\frac{\arg e-\arg\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{r}(0)}{2}\bigg)\;\mathbb{1}{\{\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{r}(0)\in\alpha_{1}\}}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle(1+o_{r}(1))\;\mathbb{E}\bigg[e^{-2F(\sigma_{c},r)}\sin\bigg(\frac{\arg\phi^{(r)}_{\sigma_{c},\epsilon}(x_{2})-\arg\phi^{(r)}_{\sigma_{c},\epsilon}(x_{1})}{2}\bigg)\sin\left(\frac{\arg\phi^{(r)}_{\sigma_{c},\epsilon}(e)-\arg\phi^{(r)}_{\sigma_{c},\epsilon}(\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{r}(\sigma_{c}))}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\times\big|(\phi^{(r)}_{\sigma_{c},\epsilon})^{\prime}(e)\big|\;\mathbb{1}{\{\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{r}(0)\in\alpha_{1}\}}\bigg],

(A.6)

where

\displaystyle F(\sigma_{c},r):=\mu_{\mathbb{A}_{r}}^{\textnormal{ND}}\big[\ell\in\mathcal{L}_{*}\textnormal{ such that }\tfrac{1}{2\pi}\varphi(\ell)\textnormal{ is odd and }\ell\cap\reflectbox{$\vec{\reflectbox{$\Gamma$}}$}{}_{r}[0,\sigma_{c}]\neq\emptyset\big].

Let us now gather the following observations.

•

By the optional stopping theorem, for
z\in\partial B(0,r)
and
1\geq R>r
, we have

\displaystyle(\phi^{(r)}_{\sigma_{c},\epsilon})^{\prime}(z)=(\phi^{(r)}_{\sigma_{c},\epsilon})^{\prime}(B^{\textnormal{ND}}(0))=\mathsf{E}_{z}\big[(\phi^{(r)}_{\sigma_{c},\epsilon})^{\prime}(B^{\textnormal{ND}}(\tau_{R}))\big],

where
B^{\textnormal{ND}}\sim\mathsf{P}_{z}
is the Brownian motion on
\mathbb{A}_{r}
started at
B^{\textnormal{ND}}(0)=z\in\partial B(0,r)

with reflective inner boundary
\partial B(0,r)
and stopped when hitting
\partial B(0,R)
at the hitting time
\tau_{R}
.
Since
\phi_{\sigma_{c},\epsilon}^{(r)}
converges to the radial Loewner flow
g_{\log c}

locally uniformly on
\mathbb{D}\setminus(\{0\}\cup\varpi[0,\log c])
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{r\to 0}\max_{z\in\partial B(0,r)}\big|(\phi^{(r)}_{\sigma_{c},\epsilon})^{\prime}(z)-\log c\big|=0.

This implies that

\displaystyle\lim_{r\to 0}\sin\bigg(\frac{\arg\phi^{(r)}_{\sigma_{c},\epsilon}(x_{2})-\arg\phi^{(r)}_{\sigma_{c},\epsilon}(x_{1})}{2}\bigg)=\sin\bigg(\frac{\arg x_{2}-\arg x_{1}}{2}\bigg).

•

By the uniform convergence, we also have

\displaystyle\lim_{r\to 0}\phi^{(r)}_{\sigma_{c},\epsilon}(\varpi(\sigma_{c}))=g_{\log c}(\varpi(c))=\exp\big(\mathrm{i}\Theta(\log c)\big),

where
(\Theta(t)\colon t\geq 0)
is the driving function of the curve
(\varpi(t)\colon t\geq 0)
as in (
3.14
).

•

By Itô’s formula, the following process is a martingale for
\varpi
:

\displaystyle S(t):=\mathrm{e}^{\frac{3}{4}t}\,|g_{t}^{\prime}(e)|\,\sin\bigg(\frac{\arg g_{t}(e)-\Theta(t)}{2}\bigg).

Plugging these observations into (
A
), by letting
r\to 0
and shrinking
\alpha_{1}
to a point,
and applying the optional stopping theorem to
S
, we find

\displaystyle\frac{1}{c}\;\sin\bigg(\frac{\arg e-\Theta(0)}{2}\bigg)=\;

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{-2F(c)}\;\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sin\bigg(\frac{\arg g_{\log c}(e)-\Theta(\log c)}{2}\bigg)\big|g^{\prime}_{\log c}(e)\big|\bigg]

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle c^{-\frac{3}{4}}\,\mathrm{e}^{-2F(c)}\;\mathbb{E}\big[S(\log c)\big]=c^{-\frac{3}{4}}\,\mathrm{e}^{-2F(c)}\;\sin\bigg(\frac{\arg e-\Theta(0)}{2}\bigg),

which by Equation (
A.2
) implies the asserted exact identity (
A.1
):

\displaystyle F(c)=\mu_{\mathbb{D}}\big[\ell\in\mathcal{L}_{*}\textnormal{ such that }\tfrac{1}{2\pi}\varphi(\ell)\textnormal{ is odd and }\ell\cap\varrho\neq\emptyset\big]=\frac{1}{8}\log c.

∎
